### BUG-040 [P3][VAL] The DDM slip rule scales a regular-size extra at a window end, and misses a slip next to a big raise
Status: OPEN
Found: iteration 29 by code review 4 (#7, #8), re-checked at 1810180 (`qa/evidence/iter29-review4-checks.txt`); moved out of BUG-017 in iteration 31 | Fixed: — | Regression test: —
Repro: (a) four regular payments plus a 1.2x extra in the last full year: the extra is scaled as if it had slipped, so growth reads 5.46%, between 5.00% without it and 8.11% with it. The iteration-25 demo's MSFT "slipped" payment was such an extra, and SCCO's 2019 (5 payments, 4 in each neighbouring year) will be scaled once it opens the window (anchor 2029). (b) a slipped ex-date in a year whose payments rose by more than half: the size test fails, so the extra payment counts in full (8.01% against a true 5.36%).
Expected: (a) under today's rule that every payment counts (Q3), only a payment shown to have slipped (a neighbouring year one short, or an ex-date at the year boundary) is scaled; (b) the slip is scaled.
Notes: no current value is affected. Until (a) is fixed, the rule partly decides Q3 for extras up to 1.5x.

### BUG-041 [P0][GATHER] Without a FRED key, every valuation used the year's first trading-day rate, not the latest
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 35)
Found: iteration 35 while tracing BUG-037 (`qa/evidence/iter35-treasury-feed-order.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 35 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_fred_handler.py::test_the_keyless_rate_is_the_feeds_latest_day`, `::test_the_treasury_feed_takes_its_latest_date_in_either_order`, `::test_a_feed_row_without_yields_gives_no_rate`
Repro: without `FRED_API_KEY`, `FREDHandler` falls back to Treasury.gov's daily yield curve CSV for the year and read its last row as the latest. The feed lists the year newest first (fetched 2026-10-20: first row 10/19/2026, 10-year 5.15%; last row 01/02/2026, 4.19%), so every key-less run used January 2's rate; in early January, with the current year's feed empty, the prior year's January 2. The golden collection of 2026-10-20 stored 0.0419 for every ticker.
Expected: the latest day's rate (5.15% on 2026-10-19).   Actual: 4.19%, so CAPM discount rates were about a point low and DCF and DDM values too high: on the golden copy 13 of 114 valuation rows and 7 of 19 summaries move with the right rate, AAPL's DCF base $140.53 -> $121.67, MSFT's $221.32 -> $191.62, SPY's DDM $142.23 -> $122.89 (`qa/evidence/iter35-bug041-impact.txt`); owner earnings didn't move (their 7% floor is above both rates).
Root cause: `src/fetchers/fred_handler.py` `_fetch_treasury_gov_year` took `lines[-1]`. There were no FREDHandler tests at all.
Fix: the row with the latest date is taken, whatever the feed's order, and its date is kept in the yields (`date`); a dated row without yields counts as no rate. Three mutants (the last row, the first row, the old metadata count) each fail a test. Live, the key-less fallback now returns 2026-10-19's 5.15% (`iter35-bug041-live.txt`).
Notes: the user's database used FRED (its JSON records `"source": "fred"`), so its values are unaffected; any run without a key, including this loop's golden collection, was affected. The golden DB in SCRATCH still holds 0.0419: re-collect it in the next sweep.

### BUG-042 [P1][GATHER] XOM resolves to ExxonMobil Holdings Corp's new CIK, which has no financial history
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 38): the gap is explained by name; whether to keep the predecessor's history is Q6
Found: iteration 37 via VAL-09 (lead L1; `qa/evidence/iter37-val09.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 38 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_fetcher_concurrency.py::test_a_filer_without_annual_financials_is_explained`, `::test_a_failed_facts_request_is_not_explained_as_missing_data`, `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_an_empty_sec_record_says_why`, `tests/test_web_workstation.py::test_the_fa_and_val_tabs_say_why_sec_gave_no_annual_financials`
Repro: SEC's company_tickers.json, fetched for the golden collection of 2026-10-20, maps XOM to CIK 2115436, "ExxonMobil Holdings Corp", whose filings start 2026-07-01 (S-8 POS and 8-K; no 10-K or 10-Q yet) and whose companyfacts hold 94 us-gaap concepts. The golden collection stored no annual financials for XOM (6 quarters), so every XOM model that needs fiscal years is N/A; the only hint is the generic "[data-quality/high] No annual financial data extracted." The user's August collection used Exxon Mobil Corp's CIK 34088 (20 fiscal years, cached in `data/cache`).
Expected: a ticker that moved to a new registrant keeps its predecessor's history, or the gap is explained by name.   Actual: a fresh collection has no XOM fundamentals; re-collecting into an existing database keeps the old rows but switches the stored CIK to 2115436.
Fix: when a filer's SEC data gives no annual financials, the run adds a warning naming the ticker, CIK, registrant and reason: "SEC EDGAR: no annual financials for XOM from CIK 0002115436 (ExxonMobil Holdings Corp): it has filed no annual report (10-K) yet (its filings listed from 2026-07-01)". `collection_runs` keeps each run's warnings (a new `warnings` column, added in place by the store's migration), and the FA and VAL tabs show the latest run's note. On the real-DB copy, re-collecting XOM keeps its 20 stored years and shows the note (`qa/evidence/iter38-bug042-e2e.txt`).
Notes: following the predecessor's history (a successor map, or the CIK already stored for the ticker) is a design choice, put to the user as Q6. Until the new registrant files its first 10-K, a fresh collection has no XOM fundamentals.

### BUG-043 [P1][GATHER] ARM's 20-F filings report in US GAAP, but only 10-K forms are read
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 40)
Found: iteration 37 via VAL-09 (lead L1) | Fixed: iteration 40 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_foreign_issuers_annual_report_is_read` (20-F, 20-F/A, 40-F), `::test_a_filer_reporting_in_another_currency_gets_no_record_and_a_reason`, `::test_an_empty_sec_record_says_why`, `tests/test_unmapped.py::test_a_foreign_issuers_annual_report_is_scanned_too`
Repro: `src/parsers/xbrl_parser.py` reads `_ANNUAL_FORMS = {"10-K", "10-K/A"}` and `_QUARTERLY_FORMS` (10-Q and 10-K). ARM files 20-F with us-gaap facts (288 concepts; its revenue and net-income facts come from 20-F (18) and 6-K (60) filings), so its golden collection stored no annual or quarterly financials and no SEC-based model can value it.
Expected: a 20-F (or 40-F) filer that tags us-gaap facts gets its annual financials.   Actual: none, and no warning (BUG-044).
Notes: since iteration 38 the run says why: "it files as a foreign issuer (20-F, 6-K); only 10-K and 10-Q filings are read".

Fix: a foreign issuer's annual report (20-F, 20-F/A, 40-F, 40-F/A) counts as annual for the annual record, the vintages and the unmapped-tag worklist. A filer reporting in another currency would then resolve only its share counts (the parser reads each field in its dollar unit, so nothing is mixed), so a record without a single dollar figure is dropped and the run explains it: "it reports in CNY (20-F), which isn't converted to dollars". A 6-K-only filer reads "it has filed no annual report (20-F) yet". Impact (`qa/evidence/iter40-bug043-impact.txt`): of 143 cached companyfacts files (the user's 126 and the golden basket's), 142 records are unchanged and ARM goes from 0 to 6 fiscal years. A live ARM collection (`iter40-bug043-e2e.txt`) stores FY2021–26 (FY2025: revenue $4,007M, net income $792M, diluted EPS 0.75, as reported) and values it: DCF $15.27, Graham $12.18, Lynch $10.21, multiples $151.28 (median $13.73 against $228.05); the oracle agrees at both levels (0 of 4 beyond 0.5%), and the FA tab shows the six years with no gap note. Six mutants each fail a test.
Notes: ARM's 6-K interim reports still aren't read, so it has no quarterly or TTM rows (BUG-058).
Iteration 48: code review 6 found that 20-F records reach the models without the ADS ratio, and convenience translations get non-USD reporters in (BUG-065); a move to IFRS leaves a stale record (BUG-066).
### BUG-044 [P2][GATHER] A filer whose SEC data yields no financials gets no explanation (TSM, ARM)
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 38)
Found: iteration 37 via VAL-09 (lead L1) | Fixed: iteration 38, with BUG-042 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_an_empty_sec_record_says_why`, `::test_a_10k_filer_whose_facts_cover_no_full_year_gets_the_last_reason`, `tests/test_sqlite_store.py::test_a_collection_run_keeps_its_warnings`, `tests/test_web_workstation.py::test_the_fa_and_val_tabs_say_why_sec_gave_no_annual_financials`, `::test_the_tabs_render_on_a_database_from_before_runs_kept_warnings`, `tests/test_web_api_quality.py::test_runs_give_their_warnings_as_a_list`
Repro: TSM files 20-F in IFRS (`ifrs-full`, 334 concepts; no `us-gaap`), which the canonical map doesn't cover, so its golden collection stored no SEC financials and recorded no warning at all; ARM likewise (BUG-043), and XOM's warning is the generic data-quality one (BUG-042).
Expected: GATHER-02's "graceful, explained gap", e.g. "SEC financials not mapped: IFRS filer (20-F)", naming the form and taxonomy.   Actual: an empty FA tab and N/A models with only "insufficient history" as the reason.
Fix: BUG-042's warning names the cause: "it reports in IFRS (20-F, 6-K), which isn't mapped" (TSM), "it files as a foreign issuer (20-F, 6-K); only 10-K and 10-Q filings are read" (ARM), "SEC has no XBRL financial statements for it", "its companyfacts request failed (…)", or as a last resort "its 10-K facts give no full fiscal year of a mapped concept". It reaches the run's JSON warnings, `collection_runs.warnings` (a list in `GET /api/quality/runs`; databases without the column still render), and the FA and VAL tabs: checked live on port 8765 and in Chrome (`iter38-bug042-e2e.txt`). Twelve mutants each fail a test (`iter38-bug042-mutants.txt`).

Iteration 40: with 20-F read (BUG-043), ARM's reason is gone; a 6-K-only filer reads "it has filed no annual report (20-F) yet", and a filer reporting in another currency "it reports in CNY (20-F), which isn't converted to dollars".
### BUG-045 [P2][GATHER] A failed companyfacts or submissions request is not recorded as an error
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 41)
Found: iteration 38 while fixing BUG-042 | Fixed: iteration 41 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_fetcher_concurrency.py::test_a_failed_sec_request_is_an_error` (companyfacts, submissions), `::test_the_run_summary_counts_a_failed_sec_request_as_an_error`, `tests/test_sec_cache.py::test_a_failed_request_is_not_logged_as_a_success`
Repro: `SECHandler.fetch_all` stores a failed request's message as `company_facts_error` or `submissions_error` (a 403 for a blocked User-Agent, a 5xx, a timeout), but nothing reads those keys: `StockData.merge_sec_data` handles only `error`, `cik` and `submissions`. The ticker's record is empty, its `errors` list stays empty, and the run summary counts it under "Successfully fetched" (`summary["successful"]` counts tickers without errors). Since iteration 38 a failed companyfacts request at least shows as a warning ("its companyfacts request failed (…)"); a failed submissions request is still silent.
Expected (GATHER-06): a failed request is an error for that ticker, and the summary is honest.   Actual: a success, with at most a warning.

Fix: `StockData.merge_sec_data` records each failed part as an error ("SEC EDGAR companyfacts: …", "SEC EDGAR submissions: …"), so `error_count` and the run summary count it, and `SECHandler.fetch_all` logs "SEC data for T is incomplete" instead of "Successfully fetched SEC data" after a failed part. The exporters don't branch on errors, so nothing else changes. The real CLI (`src.main.main`) with companyfacts blocked by a stubbed 403 now prints "Successfully fetched: 0 / With errors: 1" (before: 1 and 0), stores `error_count` 1 and the error in the JSON, and the FA note names the 403 (`qa/evidence/iter41-bug045-e2e.txt`). Three mutants each fail a test.
Notes: that run still exits 0 with every ticker failed: lead L18 (CLI-01).
Iteration 48: code review 6 found that on this failure path a failed submissions request also wipes a financial company's sector ratios (BUG-064).

The settings exist only as `STOCK_WEB_*` environment variables (`src/webapp/settings.py`), which nothing on the command line lists; the `__main__` docstring names four of the five (not `STOCK_WEB_ALLOW_QUOTE_REFRESH`).
Expected: `--help` prints usage with the environment variables, and unknown arguments are refused.   Actual: a server starts. (This loop's lookup started one against the user's database; it served no request, was stopped, and `stock.db` and its WAL are unmodified.)

### BUG-047 [P2][VAL] A declared split made after the latest 10-K can explain an older merger or IPO seam, and is then applied twice
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 42)
Found: iteration 39 by code review 5 (I1), re-run on 98013f4 (`qa/evidence/iter39-review5-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 42 (see LOG) | Regression test: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_a_split_after_the_latest_filing_never_explains_an_older_seam`
Repro: shares double in FY2020 through a stock-paid merger while EPS keeps rising (2.3 → 2.6), and a 2:1 split follows the FY2025 10-K (`review5/demo_double_split.py`): FY2016–19 get split_factor 4.0 and EPS 0.50–0.575, against 2.0 and 1.00–1.15 at 61f8a56. End to end (`t_split/build_db.py`), Lynch's base is $18.60 on 13.4% growth against $7.75 on 5.0%, and the oracle flags it (L2 MISMATCH, 58.3%). Variant (`demo_old_split.py`): a split whose own seam lies before the 10-FY window is taken by a merger seam in the window's first year (FY2016's EPS halved, 2.0 → 1.0); there the oracle's closeness heuristic agrees with the code.
Expected: a split restates only the years filed before it; a merger seam stays a merger.   Actual: `_declared_run` offers every split dated after the older year's period end, and since a8fb8e4 (BUG-031's fix) a declared match skips the EPS-direction test; `_apply_later_splits` then applies the post-filing split a second time.
Impact today: none. No post-filing split matches a seam on the real or golden copies; CRWD is the near miss (IPO seam 3.122 against its 4:1 of 2026-07-02: ×1.281, tolerance ×1.25).
Notes: the review's trial patch (drop splits dated after the latest FY filing before `_normalize_splits`) fixes the main case with no current value moving. The variant needs another guard: row-level `filed_date` can't decide it (13 of 23 real matched seams fall outside the two years' filing dates).

Fix: `load_inputs` offers `_normalize_splits` only the splits dated on or before the latest filing, the complement of what `_apply_later_splits` applies, so a post-filing split moves every year once and never explains a seam. The review's case end to end (`t_split/build_db.py`): Lynch $7.75 on 5.0% growth (was $18.60 on 13.4%), and the oracle agrees at both levels (it flagged 58.3% before). Impact: 0 of 762 real and 0 of 114 golden valuation rows change (both copies backfilled with 9892af9's code and with the fix; `qa/evidence/iter42-bug047-impact.txt`). Two mutants each fail a test.
Notes: the variant, a split whose own seam lies before the window, is a different mechanism: BUG-059.
### BUG-048 [P2][VAL] After a failed quote, a split made since the last priced snapshot moves per-share values off the shown price's basis
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 43)
Found: iteration 39 by code review 5 (I2), re-run on 98013f4 (`qa/evidence/iter39-review5-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 43 (oracle rule 30b3c5a, then the fix; see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_a_split_after_the_latest_price_leaves_every_figure_on_that_prices_basis`, `::test_a_split_before_the_latest_price_leaves_yahoos_figures_as_they_are`
Repro: FY2023–25 filed 2025-08-08, a 10:1 split on 2026-06-12, and the latest priced snapshot from 2026-06-01 at $1,000: a later run's quote failed, so BUG-033's fix wrote no snapshot, while that run still stored the split and the price bars (`t_split/stale_snapshot.py`). Latest EPS 3.00, split_factor 10.0, Graham base $14.21 against the $1,000 price (61f8a56: EPS 30.00, $142.08). With Yahoo's split-adjusted dividends, DDM is $9.45 at both revisions, so DDM was already on the new basis; BUG-036's fix moved Graham, Lynch, the multiples band and the DCF there too. The verdict reads "expensive", with upside near −90%, until a run gets a quote.
Expected: per-share values on the price's basis: apply a post-filing split only up to the snapshot's date, or withhold the verdict and upside.   Actual: values about a tenth of the price beside them.
Impact today: none (no split of 3:2 or more postdates the latest priced snapshot on the real or golden copies).
Notes: the oracle applies simple ratios unconditionally too, so its rule changes first (its own commit, before the fix).

Fix: values stay on the basis of the price they are compared with. `load_inputs` takes the latest priced snapshot's day; `_apply_later_splits` applies a post-filing split only up to that day, and Yahoo's dividends and FY-end price bars, adjusted for every split it knew when fetched, are multiplied back by any split dated after both the latest filing and that day. The oracle took the rule first (30b3c5a): on the scenario valued by the unfixed code it flagged all five applicable models (10x), and its verdicts on the real and golden copies didn't change. The whole scenario (`iter43_stale_db.py`: 10 FYs, dividends, bars) valued by the fix: median $277.31 beside the $1,000 price (was $27.73), and the oracle agrees at both levels (`qa/evidence/iter43-bug048-{oracle,e2e}.txt`). Impact: 0 of 762 real and 0 of 114 golden valuation rows change. Three mutants each fail a test.
Notes: a split between the price's day and the latest filing, which the filing restated, is a different case: BUG-060.
### BUG-049 [P2][VAL] BUG-017's third fix reads a special dividend next to a window end as a schedule change
Status: NEEDS-USER (§10: BUG-017 has had three fixes; how DDM reads the payment history is your decision)
Found: iteration 39 by code review 5 (I3), re-run on 98013f4 (`qa/evidence/iter39-review5-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: — | Regression test: —
Repro: a quarterly dividend growing 5% a year, with 2026's March payment ex-dated 2025-12-31 (`t_bug017/s3_min.py`), reads 5.12% (DDM base $125.23). Add a 3× special in 2024 and HEAD reads 7.75% ($139.97), against 5.12% at 61f8a56: the adjacent-year condition (e84da96) counts the special as a payment, so 2025's slip is no longer scaled. Shapes misread at both revisions, true growth 5% (`ddm_shapes.py`): semiannual to quarterly with a slip, 7.75%; quarterly to monthly with a slip, 5.98%; annual to semiannual from November 2025 with the transition year last, 9.84% (DDM $150.78 against $125); semiannual 40/60 with a slip, 4.36%. The oracle mirrors the rule and gives the same numbers, so it can't see any of them.
Impact today: none (every DDM value is the same at 61f8a56 and 98013f4: 99 real and 14 golden payers).
Analysis: three fixes (iterations 16, 25, 31) each patched the count-based reading of Yahoo's ex-dates, and each later review found a shape it misreads. Counting payments can't tell a slipped payment from an extra or special one, or a frequency change from a slip, without the declared schedule.
Options: (a) take DDM's growth history from SEC's annual dividends per share (the `dividends_per_share` field, from the 10-K's declared or cash-paid series, restated for splits), keeping Yahoo's payments for the latest rate; it needs a fallback where a filer tags only point-in-time payments (9 such years are empty after BUG-013); (b) keep only the initiation-year rule and accept slip noise of about a point of growth in the shapes above; (c) a fourth patch: count only regular-size payments in the adjacent-year test (the review's trial patch fixes the special case, and no current value moves), leaving the frequency-change shapes. Recommendation: (a). Related: Q3 (specials) and BUG-040.

### BUG-057 [P3][VALID] A period without a deciding SEC frame takes its calendar label in hash order
Status: OPEN
Found: iteration 40, while measuring BUG-043's impact (`qa/evidence/iter40-bug043-impact.txt`) | Fixed: — | Regression test: —
Repro: `XBRLParser._calendar_from_frames` returns the first entry of a list built from a set of SEC frames, so with no full-year frame (annual) or several quarter frames (quarterly) the choice follows Python's per-process string hashing. Over the 143 cached companyfacts files: 13 of 8,615 vintage periods and 8,192 of 9,789 quarterly periods get a run-dependent `frame` label (the JSON export writes it), and 3 vintage periods a run-dependent stored `calendar_year`: WMT's FY2012 vintage (accession 0001193125-12-134679, in both caches) and TJX's (0001193125-15-114276) come out 2011 or 2012 (candidates CY2011Q4I and CY2012Q4I; SEC's convention gives 2011). Annual records are unaffected (0 of 2,581).
Expected: the same input gives the same record (prefer the frame matching the period end, then a sorted order).   Actual: re-collecting can change those vintages' `calendar_year` and the exported frame labels.

Seen again in iteration 88: two offline collections of the same cached data differ in exactly WMT's and TJX's FY2012 vintages' `calendar_year` (2012, then 2011), and 13 vintage periods at 11 filers have candidates in more than one calendar year or quarter, none in the annual or quarterly records (`qa/evidence/iter88-bug127-realdata.txt` sections 5-6).

### BUG-059 [P2][VAL] A declared split whose own seam lies before the 10-FY window can explain a merger or IPO seam inside it
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 44)
Found: iteration 39 by code review 5 (I1's variant); split off in iteration 42 | Fixed: iteration 44 (oracle rule fd449ef, then the fix; see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_a_split_every_stored_year_already_reflects_explains_no_seam`, `::test_a_split_between_two_years_filings_still_explains_their_seam`
Repro (`review5/demo_old_split.py`): a 2:1 split in March 2017, restated by the FY2017 10-K, so every stored year from FY2016 is on the post-split basis, and a stock-paid merger that doubles the share count in FY2017 while EPS rises (2.0 → 2.1). `_declared_run` offers the split to the FY2016 → FY2017 seam (it is dated after FY2016's period end), and since a8fb8e4 a declared match skips the EPS-direction test, so FY2016's EPS is halved (2.0 → 1.0), still after BUG-047's fix; 61f8a56 kept it. End to end (`t_split/build_db_v2.py`, per the review): Lynch $34.80 against $14.50. The oracle's closeness heuristic agrees with the code, so it can't catch this.
Expected: a seam the split can't have made stays unadjusted.   Actual: the merger seam takes the split.
Impact today: none. On the real and golden copies the only declared match the EPS test would refuse is NOW's genuine 5:1 seam (FY2022 → FY2023: its EPS grew about 5× that year, the reason for a8fb8e4, BUG-031) (`qa/evidence/iter42-bug059-scan.txt`).
Notes: fix direction: per-field filing dates. The vintages hold each field's filing, so a split can explain a seam only if the older year's share count was last filed before the split: that keeps NOW (FY2022 last filed early 2025, before December 2025's split) and refuses the variant (FY2016 last filed in 2019, after March 2017's). The oracle rule changes first.

Fix: a filing restates its figures for every share split made before it, so `load_inputs` reads when each year's share figure was last filed (the latest vintage carrying the field that detects seams), and `_normalize_splits` offers a simple-ratio share split to a seam only if it lies between the two years' filings (`_filed_between`). Spin-off and merger price factors (not simple ratios) and databases without vintages keep the old reading. Feasibility first (`qa/evidence/iter44-bug059-seam-dates.txt`): every simple-ratio seam the code matches lies between its filings, 22 on the real copy and 8 on the golden one; the one outside is RTX's 1.589 spin-off factor (L12), which the rule leaves alone. The oracle took the rule first (fd449ef): it now reads each figure's filing date for simple splits, flagged the unfixed code (Lynch stored $34.80 against $14.50), and its output on the real and golden copies stayed identical. The variant with vintages (`iter44_variant_db.py`) valued by the fix: Lynch $14.50 on 4.2% growth (was $34.80 on 12.6%), and the oracle agrees at both levels. Impact: 0 of 762 real and 0 of 114 golden valuation rows change. Three mutants each fail a test (one by the control).
### BUG-060 [P2][VAL] A split between the latest price's day and the latest filing leaves per-share values past the split beside the older price
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 45)
Found: iteration 43, while fixing BUG-048 (`qa/evidence/iter43-bug060-repro.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 45 (oracle rule 5070282, then the fix; see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_a_split_the_filing_restated_after_the_price_is_taken_back_out`, `::test_a_split_the_filing_restated_before_the_price_stays`
Repro (`iter43_residual_db.py`): the latest priced snapshot is from 2026-01-10 ($1,000), a 10:1 split follows on 2026-02-01, and the FY2025 10-K filed 2026-02-20 restates FY2023-25 for it; a run on 2026-03-01 whose quote failed stored the 10-K, the split and Yahoo's split-adjusted dividends and bars. `_normalize_splits` moves FY2016-22 onto the restated basis, so every model comes out on the post-split basis: median $27.73 beside the pre-split $1,000. The oracle agrees with the code (0 of 5 beyond 0.5%): its closeness test reads the snapshot's count as today's basis, which it isn't here.
Expected: values on the price's basis (about $277), as for BUG-048.   Actual: a tenth of that; the verdict reads expensive and upside about -97%.
Impact today: none (no split of 3:2 or more postdates the latest priced snapshot on the real or golden copies; iteration 39's check).
Notes: fix direction: after normalizing, take back each simple share split dated between the price's day and the filing (EPS and FFO times it, shares divided), and multiply Yahoo's series back by every split after the price's day; the oracle's closeness test then needs today's basis count (the snapshot's times those splits). Oracle first.

Fix: `_undo_splits_after_price` takes every share split (a simple ratio of at least 3-for-2 or 1-for-2) dated after the price's day and on or before the latest filing back out of each year once normalized (EPS and FFO times it, shares and the split factor divided), and Yahoo's dividends and FY-end bars are taken back for every split after the price's day, not only one after the filing too. The oracle took the rule first (5070282): it moves the snapshot's count to today's basis by those splits for its closeness reading and takes every year back at the end, so its two readings (by filing date and by today's count) now agree; on the scenario valued by the unfixed code, with and without vintages, it flagged all five applicable models (10x), and its output on the real and golden copies and on BUG-048's and BUG-059's scenarios was unchanged. The scenario valued by the fix (`iter45_residual_db.py`, with and without vintages): median $277.31 beside the $1,000 price (was $27.73), and the oracle agrees at both levels; BUG-048's and BUG-059's scenarios still agree (`qa/evidence/iter45-bug060-{oracle,e2e}.txt`). Impact: 0 of 762 real and 0 of 114 golden valuation rows change. Three mutants each fail a test (one by the control).
### BUG-061 [P2][VALID] A later filing's tagging error in a comparative year overrides the figure earlier filings agree on
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 51)
Found: iteration 47 via VALID-02, -04 and -12 (`qa/evidence/iter47-{valid-sweep-real,vintage-flips,valid-triage}.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 51 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_later_filings_scale_typo_keeps_the_figure_earlier_filings_report`, `::test_a_later_filing_that_corrects_a_scale_typo_wins`, `::test_a_later_filings_zero_keeps_the_reported_total`, `::test_a_later_zero_for_a_line_item_is_kept`, `::test_a_sign_flip_goes_back_only_when_the_neighbouring_years_agree`, `::test_flipped_cash_flow_sections_go_back_when_the_year_then_adds_up`, `::test_a_ten_for_one_restatement_is_left_alone`, `::test_earlier_filings_that_disagree_leave_the_latest_figure`, `::test_a_later_filing_that_misscales_two_years_has_both_put_back`, `::test_a_fourth_quarter_comes_from_the_restored_year`, `::test_a_year_end_balance_comes_from_the_restored_year`, `tests/test_fetcher_concurrency.py::test_a_tagging_error_put_right_is_a_warning`
Repro (today's parser on the user's cached companyfacts, and the stored values): AMD FY2021 investing and financing cash flows are -$686M and -$1,895M in the 10-Ks filed 2022-02-03 and 2023-02-27, and +$686M and +$1,895M in the one filed 2024-01-31; the latest wins, so the year's sections sum to $6.10B against its $0.94B net change. STX FY2022 dividends per share: 2.77 in two filings, 2,770,000 in the one filed 2024-08-02. DIS FY2021 interest expense: $1,546M in two filings, -$1,546M in 2023's. BAC FY2008 total assets: $1,817.9B in three filings, 0 in the one filed 2011-02-25. BRK-B FY2008 weighted basic shares: 1.549M, then 1.549 trillion in 2011's. A scan of every field's vintages finds 63 field-years whose latest value is a sign flip or a power-of-ten scale of the filing before it; most are genuine (10:1 split restatements at LRCX, NVDA, NFLX, AVGO and MA; later filings correcting an earlier error at STX FY2023-24, TSLA FY2016 and LLY FY2008), and the rest are tagging errors like these (others: NEE FY2022-23 credit-loss provision, GE FY2011-12 treasury stock, TJX FY2011-12, PG impairments).
Expected: a latest value that is a pure sign flip, a power-of-ten scale or a zero of figures the earlier filings agree on (for per-share and share fields, not explained by a declared split) is read as a tagging error: keep the agreed figure and flag the conflict.   Actual: the latest figure is stored and shown (STX's FY2022 dividend reads $2,770,000 a share; AMD's FY2021 investing cash flow reads positive).
Impact today: no valuation input in the 10-FY window (the EPS and share-count changes there are split restatements); shown figures and the metrics built on them.

Fix: the parser still lets the latest filing win, except where its figure for a year is a tagging error of the one every earlier filing reported under the same tag: its negative, that figure times 10^3 or more, or a zero of a total (total and current assets and liabilities, equity). Then the year's own net change in cash decides for its three cash-flow sections (they must sum to it with the earlier figures and not with the later ones, under any of the four FX and discontinued-operations conventions), and the two years on each side decide the rest: for a sign flip every one of them has the earlier sign; for a scale or a zero the earlier figure is within a factor of ten of their median and the later one isn't. x10 and x100 are left alone (10:1 splits restate by exactly that), and so is a later zero of a line item (T-Mobile's history replaced MetroPCS's after the reverse merger; Prologis's FY2018 lease liability was off the balance sheet before ASC 842). Each figure put back is a run warning ("SEC EDGAR: STX FY2022 dividends_per_share is 2.77, as earlier filings report; the filing of 2024-08-02 reports 2.77e+06, which looks like a tagging error (scale x10^6)"), and the quarterly ladder reads it too, so a fourth quarter is the restored year less nine months and the year-end balance-sheet checkpoint matches. Over the 143 cached filers it puts back 14 figures at 12 (`qa/evidence/iter51-bug061-repairs.txt`, each triaged): AMD FY2021's investing and financing cash flows, STX FY2022's dividend, BAC FY2008's total assets, BRK-B FY2008's and MRK FY2010's share counts, and sign flips at GE, PEP, RTX, WMT, WELL, DE and NOW whose neighbouring years all side with the earlier filings; it keeps BRK-B FY2010's, STX FY2023-24's, TSLA FY2016's and LLY's later corrections, the 10:1 restatements, and NEE's and PG's re-signed years. Records: 14 of 143 annual and 13 quarterly digests change, no vintages (`qa/evidence/iter51-bug061-impact-parser.txt`); through the fetcher offline, AMD's TTM cash flows, STX's TTM dividends, MRK's TTM share counts (negative before), WELL's and WMT's taxes, BAC's 2008 ROA and WMT's 2009 ROIC (25.1% to 16.5%) change (`qa/evidence/iter51-bug061-impact-records.txt`). Valuations: no input changes, by construction (`qa/evidence/iter51-bug061-impact-valuation.txt`). Twelve mutants each fail a test. The user's stored copy keeps the old figures until the tickers are re-collected (L20).

### BUG-064 [P1][GATHER] A failed SEC submissions request wipes a bank's, insurer's or REIT's stored sector ratios
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 49)
Found: iteration 48 by code review 6 (I2), re-run on 3eee7b6 (`qa/evidence/iter48-review6-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 49 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_fetcher_concurrency.py::test_a_failed_submissions_request_keeps_the_stored_sector`, `::test_a_json_only_store_also_keeps_the_sector`
Repro: the sector comes only from the submissions data (`StockData.merge_sec_data`), so when that request fails the run carries on with `sector_class` None, recomputes every stored year's metrics as a general company's and overwrites `metrics_annual` for all of them (the upsert replaces every column); `companies.sector_class` survives. PLD with submissions failing (`submissions_failure_reit.py`): FFO per share 6.22 / 6.62 / 5.82 → None, the multiples band (P/FFO) $125.73 → N/A "insufficient multiple history", summary (3 models, $70.71) → (2, $68.72). JPM (`submissions_failure_bank.py`): net interest margin 0.0216 and efficiency ratio 0.524 → None; JPM's NIM goes from 18 of 19 years to 0, PLD's FFO per share from 17 of 18, CB's combined ratio from 19 of 20 (`count_wiped.py`). The same at f0db42a: pre-existing, on BUG-045's failure path.
Expected: a failed fetch never erases stored data (BUG-018's rule): take the sector from the stored companies row, or skip the metrics write for that run.   Actual: one timeout erases a financial company's sector ratios until the next good run; 28 companies in the user's database are exposed (13 banks, 8 insurers, 7 REITs).

Fix: when the submissions data is missing, `fetch_ticker` takes the sector from the stored record before computing metrics (`_stored_sector`: the database's companies row, read-only, else the JSON export's `sector_class`) and says so in a warning ("sector 'reit' taken from the stored record"); with no stored record the run goes on as before. A sector of None with annual financials only happens when submissions failed (a `--no-sec` or CIK-not-found run has no financials). The review's demos on the fixed tree (`qa/evidence/iter49-bug064-e2e.txt`): PLD keeps FFO per share in 17 of 18 years (was 0), its P/FFO band $125.73 and its summary (3 models, $70.71); JPM keeps its net interest margin in 18 of 19 years and CB its combined ratio in 19 of 20. Three mutants each fail a test (no fallback; a lookup that ignores the ticker, caught by a second stored company; no JSON fallback).
### BUG-065 [P1][VAL] A US-GAAP 20-F filer is valued per ordinary share against a per-ADS price, and a non-USD reporter gets in through US$ convenience translations
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 50)
Found: iteration 48 by code review 6 (I1), re-run on 3eee7b6 (`qa/evidence/iter48-review6-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 50 (oracle rule 8d8154c, then the fix; see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_a_foreign_issuers_per_share_figures_move_to_the_ads_basis`, `::test_the_ads_basis_is_only_for_a_foreign_issuer_whose_count_is_a_multiple` (a 10-K filer, a 1:1 20-F), `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_filer_reporting_in_another_currency_with_dollar_convenience_figures_gets_no_record`
Repro: since BUG-043's fix (14ac7c6) 20-F records are read, but nothing knows an ADR's ratio of ordinary shares per ADS: Graham and Lynch use the filing's per-ordinary-share EPS and book value, and the DCF divides by shares after the 75% rule swaps Yahoo's ADS-equivalent count for the filing's ordinary count. ARM quoted as a 5-share ADS (`ads_demo.py`: price x5, Yahoo's count /5): DCF $15.27, Graham $12.18, Lynch $10.21 against $1,140.25 (per ADS they are 5x), median $13.73, upside -98.8%. And `_in_dollars` keeps a record that has any USD fact, while non-USD 20-F filers tag a US$ convenience translation of each year: TSM's real facts with IFRS concepts relabelled as US GAAP (`tsm_as_usgaap.py`) keep 10 fiscal years at each year's convenience rate, valued per ordinary share (25.93B) against $421.70 per ADS: median $20.40, upside -95.2%; before 14ac7c6 the same filer got an explained gap. Many large ADRs file US-GAAP 20-Fs in renminbi with convenience translations and ADS ratios other than 1 (BABA, PDD, BIDU). The oracle copies the 75% rule and agrees with the code on both demos.
Expected: per-share inputs on the ADS basis (or the per-share models N/A with the ratio as the reason), and convenience translations treated as such.   Actual: values off by the ADS ratio, shown with a verdict. No stored ticker today (ARM's ADS is one share; TSM files IFRS).
Notes: fix direction (review): for filers whose latest annual report is a 20-F or 40-F, compare Yahoo's count (and market cap ÷ price) with the filing's count; a simple ratio of 1.5 or more is an ADS ratio: rescale the per-share inputs and record `ads_ratio`, and keep the 75% rule from swapping for these filers. A USD fact reported beside the same concept in another currency is a convenience translation: dropping it (the record then falls through to "it reports in TWD") or keeping it flagged is a policy choice. Oracle rule first.

Fix: (1) the ADS basis: for a filer whose latest annual report is a 20-F or 40-F, a filed share count within 5% of a whole multiple n >= 2 of Yahoo's count (or of 1/n) is the ADS ratio, and `load_inputs` moves every year's per-share figures to the ADS basis (EPS and FFO times n, shares divided) before the share count is chosen, so the 75% rule compares like with like; each applicable model's assumptions record `ads_ratio`. (2) Convenience translations: the parser reads a filer's reporting currency as the one most of its monetary facts are in, so a filer reporting in TWD or CNY gets no annual record, vintages or quarters even beside some USD facts, and the run explains "it reports in TWD (…), which isn't converted to dollars" (dropping them keeps the existing stance; valuing at convenience rates would be a new feature). The oracle took the ADS rule first (8d8154c): on the 5-share ADS demo valued by the unfixed code it flagged DCF, Graham and Lynch (76.36, 60.91, 51.04 per ADS against 15.27, 12.18, 10.21 stored), and its output elsewhere was unchanged. Valued by the fix (`qa/evidence/iter50-bug065-e2e.txt`): 76.36 / 60.91 / 51.04 and multiples 756.40, upside -94.0% (the 1:1 original's), the oracle agreeing; TSM relabelled as US GAAP keeps 0 fiscal years with its convenience translations, and only its DDM (on Yahoo's per-ADS dividends) applies. Impact: 0 of 143 cached companyfacts records change; 0 of 762 real, 114 golden and 6 ARM valuation rows. Six mutants each fail a test.
### BUG-066 [P2][GATHER] A filer whose US-GAAP annual reports stop (it moved to IFRS) keeps a stale record with no note
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 55)
Found: iteration 48 by code review 6 (I3), re-run on 3eee7b6 (`qa/evidence/iter48-review6-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 55 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_record_that_stops_where_ifrs_reports_begin_is_explained`, `::test_ifrs_facts_that_are_no_later_annual_report_say_nothing` (no later IFRS year; later 6-K interims only), `tests/test_fetcher_concurrency.py::test_a_record_that_stops_where_ifrs_reports_begin_is_a_warning`, `tests/test_web_workstation.py::test_the_fa_and_val_tabs_say_when_annual_financials_stop`
Repro: TSM's real facts with fiscal years to 2019 as US GAAP and later ones left in IFRS (`ifrs_switcher.py`): FY2015-19 are kept and valued beside today's price, Graham on FY2017 ($4.44) and the multiples band on FY2019 ($33.18), computed 2026-10-20, with no note; `explain_no_annual_financials` runs only for an empty record. Before 14ac7c6 the filer got an explained empty record. The same gap exists for a 10-K filer that moved to an IFRS 20-F.
Expected: a note when ifrs-full holds annual-report facts for periods after the record's latest fiscal year ("annual financials stop at FY2019: later annual reports are in IFRS, which isn't mapped"), shown on the FA and VAL tabs.   Actual: nothing says the figures are years old except the "Basis FY" column. Whether models should go N/A on a stale basis is Q8.

Fix: `explain_stale_annual_financials` says why a non-empty record stops: ifrs-full annual-report facts (20-F, 40-F, 10-K) for a period ending after the record's last one ("later annual reports are in IFRS (20-F), which isn't mapped"; comparatives and 6-K interims don't count). The run records it as "SEC EDGAR: annual financials stop at FY2019 for TSM: ...", and the FA and VAL tabs show it, as they show the empty-record note. Whether models go N/A on such a basis stays Q8. No cached filer gets the note: the only one with IFRS facts, TSM, has no US-GAAP record, which is already explained (`qa/evidence/iter55-bug066-impact.txt`). End to end: ARM's real facts with everything filed after its FY2024 20-F moved to ifrs-full keep FY2021-24, the run's note says they stop at FY2024, and both tabs show it (`qa/evidence/iter55-bug066-e2e.txt`; review 6's TSM demo no longer applies, since BUG-065 reads its renamed TWD facts as a TWD reporter). Five mutants each fail a test.

### BUG-070 [P3][DOCS] Labels and docs lag the code: "fred_treasury_gov" without FRED, the README's rate example, "annual (10-K)"
Status: OPEN
Found: iteration 48 by code review 6 (M5, M7), re-run on 3eee7b6 (`qa/evidence/iter48-review6-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: — | Regression test: —
Repro (`quality_sources.py`): the data-sources label reads "fred_treasury_gov" (or "fred_estimate") on /quality although FRED wasn't consulted; README.md's rate JSON example (around line 204) has no `date` and shows a `series` key nothing writes; README.md (around line 270) says "Official financial statements (10-K, 10-Q)" and an `xbrl_parser.py` docstring "annual (10-K)" after 20-F and 40-F were added.

### BUG-074 [P2][VALID] Quarterly weighted-average share counts are differences of year-to-date averages
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 56)
Found: iteration 51, checking BUG-061's impact on MRK's TTM rows (`qa/evidence/iter51-bug074-repro.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 56 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_quarters_weighted_share_count_is_its_own_average`, `::test_a_quarters_own_3_month_share_count_is_used_where_filed`, `::test_a_quarters_own_share_count_fills_a_gap_in_the_year_to_date_rungs`, `tests/test_ttm.py::test_ttm_share_counts_are_the_four_quarters_average`
Repro: the quarterly ladder (`XBRLParser._collect_discrete_flows`) differences every duration field's year-to-date facts, share counts included: AAPL's Q2-Q4 FY2025 diluted weighted shares read -0.047B, -0.052B and -0.047B, MSFT's -0.001B to -0.006B, MRK's FY2011 +0.002B, -0.004B and -0.008B; only Q1, a 3-month fact, is right. A TTM row sums the four quarters, which telescopes to roughly a count (AAPL 14.703B) without being the trailing year's average.
Expected: a quarter's weighted-average count is its own 3-month figure (10-Qs tag it), and a TTM count an average of the four, not their sum.   Actual: near-zero and negative counts in `financials_quarterly`, the `/api/companies/{ticker}/financials/quarterly` endpoint and the CSV and JSON exports.
Impact today: no valuation input (the models read annual rows) and no row of the statements tab (it shows EPS, not share counts); every company's stored quarterly share counts and its TTM count.

Fix: a weighted-average share count isn't additive, so the quarterly ladder no longer differences it. A quarter takes its own 3-month figure, which 10-Qs tag, even where the year-to-date rungs skip it. Where no such figure exists (Q4: 10-Ks don't tag one), the quarter's average is the year-to-date share-days less the previous rung's, over the quarter's days. A TTM count is the four quarters' average, not their sum. The share-days formula, checked first against the filed 3-month figures, put 93.6% of 5,613 quarters within 0.5% of them (median 0.006%), but year-to-date rungs that disagree (a later 10-Q's restatement, a mis-scaled fact) still left 69 beyond 10%. Hence the filed figure first: 99.9% of 5,779 quarters within 0.5%; the 6 still beyond 10% are restatements where the parser takes a later filing's figure (MA's and DUK's splits, TMUS's merger) and a scale mix-up in the check's own pick (`qa/evidence/iter56-bug074-check.txt`). Over every cached filer, quarters more than 2x off their fiscal year's average fall from 5,659 of 7,502 (75.4%) to 253 of 7,615 (3.3%), what's left being split years (BUG-082) and mis-scaled facts. Through the fetcher's pipeline (share-scale step included), for 23 split-heavy tickers, quarters off by 2x fall from 1,086 of 1,529 to 140 of 1,559, and TTM counts beyond 10% of their year's average at fiscal year ends from 42 of 357 to 32 (`qa/evidence/iter56-bug074-impact.txt`). Nothing else moves: no annual value, no metric, no valuation (the models read annual rows); 15 false share-scale rescales of near-zero quarterly counts are gone, and two mis-scaled period-end counts now rescale right (AMT, O). Six mutants each fail a test.
### BUG-075 [P0][VAL] The multiples band's FY-end prices are Yahoo's dividend-adjusted closes, not the market's
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 53)
Found: iteration 51 by code review 7 (C1), re-run in iteration 52 (`qa/evidence/iter52-review7-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 53 (oracle rule 5c1b693, then the fix; see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_yahoo_new_sections.py::test_price_bars_are_market_closes_not_dividend_adjusted`, `tests/test_store_migration.py::test_bars_stored_before_market_closes_are_flagged_dividend_adjusted`, `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_fy_end_prices_are_market_closes` (flagged, before the flag, market closes)
Repro: `YahooHandler._get_price_bars` calls `stock.history(period="max", interval="1d")`, and yfinance 1.0 defaults to `auto_adjust=True` (OHLC adjusted for dividends as well as splits), so `price_bars.close` holds total-return prices, which `load_inputs` reads as each FY-end price for `value_multiples`. Rebuilt from the stored bars and Yahoo's dividends (inverting Yahoo's factor 1 - d / prior close, newest first), the market closes are exactly the published ones: KO 2021-12-31 stored 51.854 in the August copy and 51.546 in the October one, against 59.21; JPM 140.571 against 158.35; AAPL 2021-09-24 143.425 against 146.92; MO 33.419 against 47.39. A past day's stored close falls with every later dividend, so it changes from one collection to the next, and the HP tab and `/api/export/stock/{T}/bars.csv` call it "close" (KO's row: 51.546).
Expected: the spec's "multiple = FY-end price (nearest `price_bars` close on or before fiscal year end)" and VAL-05's "each year's price taken at its fiscal-year end with no look-ahead": the market close on today's split basis.   Actual: a price lowered by every dividend paid since: 98 of 118 applicable multiples rows in the user's copy understate the base value by more than 0.5% (52 by more than 5%, 17 by more than 10%; MO $45.05 against $57.66, CVX $79.81 against $95.58, JPM $188.48 against $212.32, KO $70.06 against $76.94); 20 of 127 headline medians move beyond 0.5% (C +17.8%, MS +16.5%, JPM +12.6%, WFC +11.9%, GS +11.7%, MO +10.9%, O +10.7%); USB reads expensive, and fair with market closes.
Impact today: every dividend payer's multiples value and the medians and verdicts built on it. The oracle reads the same column and VAL-13's hand-worked check the same closes, so VAL-05's and VAL-13's PASS hid it (both now FAIL→BUG-075). The spec already says what the price is, so this is a fix, not a policy question: the oracle's rule first.

Fix: the fetch asks for market prices (`history(..., auto_adjust=False)`: Yahoo's split-adjusted, not dividend-adjusted, OHLC). Bars already stored hold Yahoo's adjusted prices, so the migration flags them `price_bars.dividend_adjusted = 1` once, as the column arrives, and a re-collected ticker's bars are market prices again (the flag cleared). For a flagged bar, or any bar of a database from before the flag, `load_inputs` rebuilds the FY-end market close by undoing Yahoo's factor 1 - d / (the close before the ex-date) for each later dividend, newest first. The user's stored valuations come right on the next backfill or collection, without re-collecting prices; the HP tab and bars.csv show the stored bars, which stay Yahoo's adjusted prices until the ticker is re-collected (L20). The oracle took the rule first (5c1b693). Code and oracle share the rebuild, so it was probed before either used it (section 7): against Yahoo's unadjusted closes fetched live for the golden basket, all 18 tickers' FY-end bars rebuild within 0.0001%, and every stored day within 0.02% at the 99th percentile (`qa/evidence/iter53-bug075-probe.txt`). On copies valued by the unfixed code, the oracle flagged 101 more multiples rows on the real data and 11 on the golden copy. Valued by the fix, they come back to the known 8 and 0 (`qa/evidence/iter53-bug075-{oracle,impact}.txt`). Changes: 103 of 600 real valuation rows, all multiples (98 beyond 0.5%, median +5.3%); 20 headline medians beyond 0.5% (C +17.8%, MS +16.5%, JPM +12.6%, WFC +11.9%, GS +11.7%, MO +10.9%, O +10.7%, PGR +9.3%, T +9.1%, COP +7.0%); USB's verdict goes from expensive to fair; on the golden copy, 12 multiples rows, and JPM's and PGR's medians (+13.2%, +6.5%). End to end, the golden basket collected live with the fix stores 209,515 market-price bars (KO 2021-12-31 at 59.21, NVDA 2023-01-27 at 20.365 on today's split basis). The oracle passes on it at both levels, VAL-13's hand-worked script equals all 14 applicable models to 0.000%, and bars.csv and the VAL API show 59.21 and KO's P/E band 23.0 / 25.31 / 29.05 (`qa/evidence/iter53-bug075-e2e.txt`). Nine mutants each fail a test.
### BUG-076 [P2][VAL] The ADS ratio misreads a drifted share count, and a listed class's count as an ADS
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 57)
Found: iteration 51 by code review 7 (I1, I2), re-run in iteration 52 (`qa/evidence/iter52-review7-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 57 (oracle rule 2de0046, then the fix; see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_an_ads_ratio_is_read_against_every_class`, `::test_the_period_end_count_reads_an_ads_ratio_a_drifted_average_misses`, `::test_an_unreadable_ads_ratio_leaves_the_per_share_models_unvalued`, `::test_an_ads_ratio_is_a_whole_number_or_unreadable` (8 cases)
Repro: `_ads_ratio` compares the latest FY's weighted diluted count with Yahoo's count of today, and the oracle copies the rule. (1) Drift: 19 of the user's 113 single-class companies' filed counts differ from Yahoo's by more than 5% (8 by more than 10%; median 1.84%). Code review 6's 5-share ADS demo with Yahoo's count 8% lower reads ads_ratio 1.0, and BUG-065's values come back (DCF 15.27, Graham 12.18, Lynch 10.21 against a $1,140.25 ADS; upside -98.8%); for an ADS of 8 or 10 shares those drifts read 9 or 11. (2) Several classes: Yahoo counts the listed one, so GOOGL relabelled 20-F reads 12.23B / 5.867B = 2.08 as an ADS of 2: Graham 90.88 → 181.75, Lynch 162.15 → 324.30, the median +23.6%, and `ads_ratio: 2` in every row's assumptions. The oracle agrees in both.
Expected: the ratio read against a count on the price's basis that covers every class (market cap ÷ price: TSM 5.186B, ARM 1.068B, GOOGL 12.228B, a ratio of 1), and per-share models N/A with a reason when a 20-F or 40-F filer's ratio isn't a whole number.   Actual: as above.
Impact today: none in the user's copy (no ADR has a record); any US-GAAP ADR collected later.

Fix: the ratio is the latest FY's period-end count (else its diluted one), on today's basis, over market cap / price, which is on the price's basis and counts every class. A ratio in [0.8, 1.25] is a 1:1 listing; otherwise a whole n >= 2, or 1/n, within 5% and a quarter of a share (so a drifted 8-share ADS isn't read as 9); otherwise it can't be read, and DCF, Graham, Lynch and owner earnings are N/A with the reason ("the ADS ratio can't be read: the latest 20-F counts 5.41 shares for each one the market counts"), while DDM (Yahoo's per-ADS dividends) and multiples (per-ADS prices over per-share EPS, times per-share EPS) don't depend on it and stay. A threshold the code and oracle share, so it was probed first on the real copy's drift, where no company has an ADS (`qa/evidence/iter57-bug076-probe.txt`): the period-end count drifts a median 1.0% from market cap / price (2.0% for the diluted count against Yahoo's); for an n-share ADS the rule misreads 1-8 of 124 and leaves 2-23 unreadable, where the old one silently misread 19-24 of 113. The oracle took the rule first (2de0046) and flagged the unfixed code on both of code review 7's demos. Valued by the fix (`qa/evidence/iter57-bug076-e2e.txt`), the drifted 5-share ADS has those four models N/A with the reason and multiples valued, the counts-agree one still reads 5, and GOOGL relabelled 20-F reads 1 (Graham 90.88, Lynch 162.15, as filed); the oracle agrees (its exit 1 there is GOOGL's DDM, underivable on the 10-K copy too). Impact: 0 of 762 real and 114 golden valuation rows change (`qa/evidence/iter57-bug076-impact.txt`). Eight mutants each fail a test.
### BUG-077 [P2][VALID] A filer's reporting currency is read over its whole history, so a switch to dollars loses the dollar years
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 58)
Found: iteration 51 by code review 7 (I3; a regression of 0ab5c81), re-run in iteration 52 (`qa/evidence/iter52-review7-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 58 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_filer_that_switched_to_dollars_keeps_its_dollar_filings`, `::test_a_filer_that_switched_from_dollars_keeps_its_dollar_years_and_says_why_they_stop`, and BUG-065's and BUG-071's currency tests
Repro: `_reporting_currency` counts every monetary us-gaap fact the filer has ever filed. A filer that reported in RUB with US$ convenience translations through FY2022 and in dollars from FY2023 (the Yandex-to-Nebius path; 146 RUB facts beside 94 USD in the synthetic case) gets no annual record, no vintages and no quarters, and the run says "it reports in RUB (20-F), which isn't converted to dollars"; f9bbc91 kept every year, the RUB years as their convenience translations. A filer that switched away from dollars keeps its later convenience translations.
Expected: the currency decided per filing (accession): the dollar filings kept, the others dropped, and the explanation only when no dollar filing is left.   Actual: as above.
Impact today: none (no cached filer changed currency: `review7/scripts/currency_margins.py`).

Fix: the currency is decided filing by filing (accession): a filing is in another currency when it holds no more USD facts than facts in some other currency, and its facts, convenience translations and share counts with them, are left out whole (`_dollar_filings`, used by the annual record, the vintages and the quarters). A filer that switched to dollars keeps its dollar filings and their restated comparatives (the RUB-to-USD case: FY2021-25, FY2022's revenue the dollar restatement, not a convenience translation; vintages from the dollar 20-Fs only); one that left dollars keeps its dollar years, and the stale-record note (BUG-066) says "later annual reports are in TWD (20-F), which isn't converted to dollars"; the empty-record explanation names the currency only when no dollar filing is left. A tie goes against dollars (a filing with as many convenience figures as its own). Before, a whole-history majority decided: with dollar facts the majority, the RUB-era convenience translations made FY2019-20 of their own (2 tests RED). No cached filer has a filing in another currency; 0 of 143 annual, vintage and quarterly digests change (`qa/evidence/iter58-bug077-impact.txt`). Six mutants each fail a test.
### BUG-078 [P2][VAL] Without an SEC filing date, Yahoo's split-adjusted dividends stay beside a pre-split price
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 61)
Found: iteration 51 by code review 7 (I4), re-run in iteration 52 (`qa/evidence/iter52-review7-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 61 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_a_split_after_the_latest_price_takes_yahoos_series_back_whatever_the_sec_record_holds` (no annual rows; rows without a filing date)
Repro: `load_inputs` puts Yahoo's dividends and FY-end bars back on the price's basis (`unpriced`, BUG-048) only when the latest annual row has a filing date (`if ratio and filed and priced_on ...`); 144dcdf dropped the `max(priced_on, filed)` the guard served but kept it, and the oracle has the same guard. A company without SEC annual financials (an IFRS filer like TSM, whose only model is DDM, or XOM's new registrant) in BUG-048's scenario (the latest priced snapshot, $1,000, before a 10:1 split that a later run stored while its quote failed) gets DDM 41.84 against 418.38 on the price's basis, and the oracle agrees with 41.84. Dropping the guard passes the whole suite.
Expected: Yahoo's series on the price's basis whatever the SEC record holds (BUG-048's Expected).   Actual: a tenth of the value.
Impact today: none in the user's copy; BUG-048's preconditions for a company without SEC annual financials.

Fix: `load_inputs` takes Yahoo's dividends and FY-end bars back to the price's basis by every split dated after the price's day, whether or not the latest annual row has a filing date: the guard 144dcdf left behind is gone, while which of those splits the filed figures restated still goes by the date (BUG-060). The oracle took the same rule first, in its own commit (2d9b9dc): on code review 7's scenario valued by the unfixed code it flags the DDM (41.84 stored, 418.4 derived, from trailing dividends of 21.98 against 2.20), and its output on the real and golden copies valued by 1d762ba and on ten earlier split, ADS and scenario databases is unchanged (`qa/evidence/iter61-bug078-oracle.txt`). Two tests RED, then GREEN: a company with no annual rows, and one whose rows have no filing date. Three mutants each fail a test: the guard back; back for the dividends only; back for the FY-end prices only, which the rows without a date catch. With the fix the scenario's DDM is 418.38, as with its dividends on the price's basis and no split stored, through `python -m src.valuation.backfill`, `/api/stocks/NOFIL/valuation` and the VAL tab of `stock-web` on a scratch copy (port 8765, collection and quote refresh off); 0 of 762 valuation rows and 0 of 127 summaries move on a copy of the user's data, 0 of 114 and 0 of 19 on the fresh golden basket, and the oracle's results there are unchanged (`qa/evidence/iter61-bug078-impact.txt`).
### BUG-079 [P3][VAL] The price's day is the collector's local date, while splits are dated in New York
Status: OPEN
Found: iteration 51 by code review 7 (M1), re-run in iteration 52 (`qa/evidence/iter52-review7-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: — | Regression test: —
Repro: `priced_on` is the snapshot's `collected_at` date, naive local time (`models/stock_data.py`, `webapp/jobs.py`), and split dates are exchange dates: the golden snapshots were stamped 02:07 on 2026-10-20 in UTC+7, 15:07 on 10-19 in New York. In BUG-048's scenario with the snapshot stamped 02:30 local on a split's ex-date (15:30 the day before in New York, before the split), the split counts as already priced: Graham 45.21, Lynch 24.76, multiples 54.67; stamped with its New York date, 452.06, 247.61 and 546.66. The oracle agrees with the first.
Expected: the price's day on the exchange's calendar.   Actual: a tenth of those values until the next priced collection.

### BUG-080 [P1][STORE] A database path holding '#' or '%xx' breaks every read-only connection: the web app answers 500 and creates an empty file
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 54)
Found: iteration 51 by code review 7 (M2, widened in the re-run), re-run in iteration 52 (`qa/evidence/iter52-review7-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 54 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_web_repository.py::test_a_database_under_any_folder_name_opens_read_only` ('#', '%41', '?' off Windows, and two controls), `::test_the_app_serves_a_database_under_a_hash_folder`, `::test_a_unc_path_keeps_its_form`, `tests/test_asof_reader.py::test_a_database_under_a_hash_folder_opens`, `tests/test_fetcher_concurrency.py::test_the_stored_sector_is_found_under_any_folder_name`
Repro: `Reader` (`webapp/repository.py:52`), `AsOfReader` (`query/asof.py:30`) and `StockDataFetcher._stored_sector` open `file:{path}?mode=ro` without escaping the path. SQLite reads '#' as the start of a fragment, so `mode=ro` is cut off with the rest of the path and the connection opens, read-write, a database at the truncated path, creating it if missing; '%41' decodes to another name ("unable to open database file"). With the golden copy under `proj#1/output/stock.db`, the web app in-process answers 500 on `/api/companies` and `/companies/KO` (200 under `plain/`) and leaves an empty file `proj` beside the folder (`qa/evidence/iter52-review7-rerun.txt`); the stored-sector fallback returns None under `data#2` and `pct%41` (BUG-064's wipe again where no JSON export exists). `Path.resolve().as_uri() + "?mode=ro"` opens all three folders.
Expected: any folder the OS allows works, and a read-only reader never writes (STORE-04).   Actual: as above.
Impact today: none for the user's path (no '#' or '%'); any install under a folder like "C#" or "100%".

Fix: one helper, `sqlite_store.read_only_uri`, builds the three read-only URIs (the web Reader, the AsOfReader and the stored-sector fallback). It escapes the three characters SQLite's URI syntax reads specially, '%', '#' and '?', and leaves the rest of the path as it is. The review's suggested `Path.as_uri()` would have broken network shares: a probe of the URI forms (`qa/evidence/iter54-bug080-uri-forms.txt`) shows the old form opening local and UNC (`\\localhost\C$\...`) paths but not '#' or '%41' folders, `as_uri()` opening local paths of every kind but no UNC path at all, and the escaped form opening all six cases, still read-only. Six tests RED, then GREEN (the '?' case runs off Windows only, which forbids the character); eight mutants each fail a test, `as_uri()` among them. In the real app (`stock-web` configured by environment on port 8765, the fresh golden collection copied under `web#1`): health, the companies API, KO's valuation, bars.csv, the statements fragment and the company page answer 200, no file appears beside the folder, and the served database's 19 tables and 220,663 rows equal their source's (`qa/evidence/iter54-bug080-realapp.txt`).
### BUG-081 [P3][CODE] 9 mutants of iterations 43-50's fixes survive the whole suite
Status: OPEN
Found: iteration 51 by code review 7 (M3), re-run in iteration 52 (`qa/evidence/iter52-review7-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: — | Regression test: —
Repro: `review7/scripts/mutants.py` on an archive of 69c5a7e, each mutant in a fresh copy with the whole suite (990 passed, 1 warning in 88.70s (0:01:28)): ads 1/n branch returns n; ads tolerance 5% -> 30%; ads: 40-F not foreign; filed_between ignores the newer date; filed_between: non-simple ratios judged too; detect date always the weighted field's; undo: non-simple factors too; unpriced without the filing guard (proposed fix); stored sector: no warning all pass. Code review 7's targeted run also has survivors for the ADS step not moving `split_factor` or `ffo_per_share`, `ads_ratio` stamped on N/A rows, and the `<`/`<=` date boundaries (BUG-072's class). One survivor changes real inputs: `_filed_between` judging non-simple ratios moves RTX's FY2016-19 split factor from 1.589 to 1.5717 on the user's copy, though no value moves at two decimals.
Expected: each fix's rule held by a test.   Actual: these rules are held by none.
Impact today: none; the next change to these rules can break them unseen.

Code review 9 (M5), re-run in iteration 63 on 51091ce, each mutant in a fresh archive with the whole suite (1037 passed each; `qa/evidence/iter63-review9-rerun.txt`): nine more survive. BUG-083's re-check under the lock and its `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (review 9: without either, the second of two concurrent first runs fails, 3 of 3), and its `except BaseException` (equivalent in practice); `unpriced` with `>=` (no test has a split on the price's day) and counting simple ratios only (none has a spin-off factor after it); BUG-076's ADS count without the split factor (a 1:1 20-F filer with a 4:1 split after its 20-F would read 0.25: Graham 4.63 and Lynch 3.98 against 18.52 and 15.92) and its 1:1 band widened to [0.7, 1.43]; BUG-074's own figures used for flows too (a design choice review 9 left alone) and the basic count summed in TTM (the test sets only the diluted one).
Code review 10 (M6), re-run in iteration 71: 13 of its 46 mutants of iterations 62-67's fixes survive the whole suite on 679a35d too (1077 passed each): m94_divide_inclusive, m94_simple_ratios_only, m94_info_none_unguarded, m84_served_any_bar, m93_no_bar_fallback, m82_year_basis_latest_rung, m82_own_no_level, m82_range_from_1_2, m92_wide_band, m92_derived_zero_kept, m92_basic_vs_diluted_eps, m92_own_nonpositive, m92_checked_tolerance_50pct. By the review's impact runs (its `survivor_impact.txt`, not re-run), four aren't equivalent: `info = stock.info` without `or {}` (under yfinance 1.0's failure, raising once and then None, the split is dropped: multiples 98.34 against 983.37), the year basis taken from the latest rung (BKNG's FY2025 Q2 EPS 27.38 becomes 1.0952), own 3-month counts kept without their split level (2,220 TTM cells in 28 tickers change, LRCX's 1.286B to 9.994B) and basic counts checked against diluted EPS (BRK-B's basic counts back to x10^6); seven are equivalent on the copies but untested, and two equivalent (`qa/evidence/iter71-review10-rerun.txt`).
### BUG-082 [P2][VALID] The quarterly ladder mixes split bases: a split restates the annual figures but not the quarters filed before it
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 62)
Found: iteration 56, checking BUG-074's remaining misses (`qa/evidence/iter56-bug082-repro.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 62 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_years_quarters_are_on_the_split_basis_its_year_is_filed_on`, `::test_a_quarters_share_counts_are_on_its_years_basis`, `::test_a_ratio_one_count_alone_shows_is_no_split`, `::test_a_reverse_mergers_restated_history_is_no_split`, `tests/test_ttm.py::test_a_trailing_year_across_a_split_is_on_its_latest_quarters_basis`, `tests/test_fetcher_concurrency.py::test_a_collected_quarterly_record_and_its_trailing_years_sit_on_one_split_basis`
Repro: each quarter's figures come from its latest filing, and a split restates only the filings after it. AMZN (20:1, June 2022): FY2020's annual EPS is the restated 2.09 and its count 10.198B, while its quarters keep the pre-split EPS 5.01, 10.31 and 12.40 and counts of 0.51B, so Q4 2020's EPS, the year less nine months, reads -25.63 for a profitable quarter. Over every cached filer, 253 of 7,615 quarterly counts stay more than 2x off their fiscal year's average, mostly from such splits (AMZN 2020, NVDA 2009). The annual record's valuation inputs put every year on the price's basis (BUG-047, BUG-048, BUG-059, BUG-060); nothing does that for the quarterly record or the TTM rows built on it.
Expected: a quarter's per-share figures and counts on one split basis with its year, and a fourth quarter derived from figures on the same basis.   Actual: mixed bases, and a fourth quarter that is the difference of two of them.
Impact today: the statements tab's quarterly and TTM views, `financials_quarterly` and `financials_ttm`, the API and the exports, for the years around a split; no valuation (the models read annual rows).

Fix: the parser reads the splits from the filings themselves (`_split_restatements`): a share count two filings give for one period at a simple ratio of 3:2 or more either way, within 0.5%, and at least two such counts in overlapping windows; a filing's basis is the product of the splits it came after. A probe on the cached filers came first (`qa/evidence/iter62-bug082-probe.txt`): of the 38 splits found, 37 are Yahoo's, and the other is Arconic's 1-for-3 of 2016, which Yahoo's history lacks; every Yahoo split since 2010 without one has no restated filing in the cache. Each quarter's per-share figures and counts then go onto the basis of its year's annual figures (that of the latest filing of the year's full-year figure, as `financials_annual` has them) before Q4 is derived; quarter-end counts onto their year end's; and a trailing year onto its latest quarter's, from the bases the parser hands over in `_split_levels`, which the fetcher pops before anything is stored. A first version took ratios within 2%, and the impact run showed it moving TMUS's MetroPCS quarters by the 2013 reverse merger (T-Mobile USA's deemed count, 1.46 to 1.49 times MetroPCS's, read as 3:2), so the tolerance went to 0.5%, which the counts' rounding allows and which keeps every real split, with a test RED first. Six tests (AMZN's own figures: Q4 2020 -25.63 and Q2 2021 -14.24 before, 0.704 and 0.7605 after), RED then GREEN; eleven mutants, ten of which fail a test, and the one that survives (a year's basis from its latest rung, not its full-year one) is equivalent in practice (`qa/evidence/iter62-bug082-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Over the 126 cached filers collected offline through the fetcher (`qa/evidence/iter62-bug082-impact.txt`): 28 change, all with splits in their filings, and no annual figure; quarterly diluted counts more than 2x off their year's 214 -> 21 (the rest scale errors, now BUG-092, IPO years, and Citigroup's 2009 issuance); quarters whose EPS exceeds the whole year's 154 -> 80, none new; nothing reaches the stored record. The README describes the basis.
### BUG-083 [P1][STORE] A first write that fails after the upgrade loses the dividend-adjusted flags for good, and BUG-075's prices return
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 60)
Found: iteration 58 by code review 8 (C1), re-run in iteration 59 (`qa/evidence/iter59-review8-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 60 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_store_migration.py::test_a_first_export_that_fails_keeps_the_flags` (a failed write, Ctrl+C), `::test_a_migration_interrupted_while_flagging_is_undone_whole`
Repro: the migration adds `price_bars.dividend_adjusted` and flags every stored bar 1 (BUG-075), but `_create_schema`'s `executescript` commits and Python's sqlite3 opens a transaction only before DML, so the `ALTER TABLE` commits at once while the `UPDATE` joins the run's writes; `export()`, `export_benchmark_bars()` and `upsert_quote()` swallow a failure and close. On a pre-flag copy of the user's data, a first export whose write fails ("database is locked") or is interrupted (Ctrl+C) leaves the column with all 1,246,064 bars unflagged, and so does every later export: KO's FY2021 price reads 51.854, not 59.21. Revalued, 98 of 118 multiples rows fall back to BUG-075's figures (MO -21.9%, CVX -16.5%, C -15.1%; KO 70.06 against 76.94, JPM 188.48 against 212.32), and the oracle, which reads the flag the same way, sees only its known 8.
Expected: the column and the flags arrive together or not at all; a bar from before the fix reads as Yahoo-adjusted whatever happens to the first run.   Actual: as above.
Impact today: the user's database has no flag column yet, so its first collection or quote refresh after the upgrade is this migration; the backfill alone never runs it.

Fix: the migration adds the column and flags the bars in one explicit transaction (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, the column re-checked once the lock is held, then the `ALTER`, the `UPDATE` and a `COMMIT`), committed before the run writes anything, and rolled back whole on any failure, Ctrl+C included. The review's alternative, flipping the encoding so an unflagged bar reads as adjusted, would also have reread every test database's bars and still leaves a row an older version rewrites misread, so the transaction is the narrower change. On pre-flag copies of the user's data (`qa/evidence/iter60-bug083-e2e.txt`): the review's abort scenario now keeps all 1,246,064 flags through a failed or interrupted first export and a later successful one (KO's FY2021 price 59.21), and the revalued copy matches the fixed one (0 of 118 multiples rows off). Hard kills of the first export's migration leave a whole copy every time, either no column (rolled back) or every bar flagged, with `integrity_check` ok, where the unfixed code killed at the same moments (0.9-1.3 s) leaves the column with every flag empty. Two mutants: running the `ALTER` outside the transaction fails the interrupted-migration test; leaving the migration's transaction open into the run's writes survives, being all or nothing as well.
### BUG-084 [P2][VAL] The market-close rebuild undoes dividends after the last stored bar, which Yahoo never applied to it
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 65)
Found: iteration 58 by code review 8 (I1), re-run in iteration 59 (`qa/evidence/iter59-review8-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 65 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_a_dividend_after_the_last_adjusted_bar_is_not_undone` (flagged bars; a database from before the flag), `::test_a_dividend_on_the_last_adjusted_bars_day_is_undone`, `tests/test_yahoo_new_sections.py::test_a_failed_price_history_request_is_a_run_warning`
Repro: for a dividend-adjusted bar `load_inputs` (and the oracle) divides by the factor of every dividend after it, but Yahoo adjusted the stored bars only for dividends up to the day they were fetched. `_get_price_bars` returns `[]` on any error, logged but not a run warning, and dividends are fetched first, so a later run can store new dividends and no bars. On a copy of the user's data given October's dividends without bars, KO's FY2021-25 FY-end prices come out +0.54% to +0.61% above the market close, JPM's +0.41% to +0.45%, XOM's +0.57% to +0.66%, and the multiples base +0.59%, +0.40% and +0.57%; each further stale quarter adds about as much. The oracle agrees with the inflated values.
Expected: only dividends up to the last flagged bar are undone, and a failed price-history request is a run warning.   Actual: as above.
Impact today: a failed bar fetch beside successful dividends, for any ticker whose bars predate BUG-075's fix.

Re-run in iteration 63 (code review 9's M6: iteration 59 took this figure from review 8 unchecked): a second stale quarter adds +0.60%, +0.45% and +0.66% to KO's, JPM's and XOM's FY2025 prices, as much as the first (`qa/evidence/iter63-review9-rerun.txt`).
Fix: Yahoo's stored bars count as adjusted for the dividends up to the last dividend-adjusted bar (the last flagged one, or any bar of a database from before the flag), which dates when Yahoo served them: `_yahoo_dividend_factors` leaves later dividends out, both as factors to undo and from the later factors each older one is solved with. And a failed price-history request is a run warning: "Yahoo Finance: price history unavailable (...); the stored daily bars weren't refreshed". The oracle took the same rule first, in its own commit (27ce753): on review 8's copy of the user's data with October's dividends and no new bars, valued by the unfixed code, it flags KO's and XOM's multiples (77.39 and 76.50 stored against 76.94 and 76.06), and with a second stale quarter JPM's too; its output elsewhere is unchanged, apart from two synthetic BUG-048 databases whose only bars are their FY ends (`qa/evidence/iter65-bug084-oracle.txt`). Four tests (two RED, one per kind of database, and the warning; one pins the boundary: a dividend on the last bar's own day is still undone), and five mutants, of which the one that survives (the cutoff from any bar, not only the flagged ones) is equivalent in practice, since a run that stores bars rewrites them all (`qa/evidence/iter65-bug084-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). With the fix the stale copies' multiples bases are 76.94, 212.32 and 76.06, the user's data's without the stale dividends, and the oracle agrees; 0 of 889 valuation and summary rows move on the user's copy and 0 of 133 on the golden basket; through the real fetcher on code review 9's fake market, a run whose price-history request fails now records the warning in its summary and in `collection_runs` (`qa/evidence/iter65-bug084-impact.txt`).
