### BUG-092 [P2][VALID] The quarterly ladder differences share counts filed at different scales, so a quarter's count comes out negative or tens of times its year's
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 66)
Found: iteration 62, checking BUG-082's impact (`qa/evidence/iter62-bug082-impact.txt`); code review 9 reported it too (M1, `qa/evidence/review9-report.md`) | Fixed: iteration 66 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_years_share_counts_are_on_one_scale_before_its_fourth_quarter` (MCD's year in millions; COP's first quarter and year in thousands), `::test_a_rung_without_eps_takes_the_scale_its_years_other_rungs_have`, `::test_a_rung_whose_eps_is_misscaled_keeps_its_count`, `::test_a_rung_whose_eps_misses_its_count_takes_its_years_scale`, `::test_a_count_at_or_below_zero_is_no_count`
Repro: a year's rungs come from their latest filings, and a later filing can re-tag a count at another scale. MCD's 10-Ks for FY2023 and FY2024 give FY2021's and FY2022's diluted counts as 751.8 and 741.3 (millions) beside nine months of 751.9M and 743.0M in units, so Q4 2021 reads -2,223.0M, Q4 2022 -2,196.7M, and their trailing years 8.17M and 8.10M against years of 751.8M and 741.3M; COP's Q1 and year of 2019 are in thousands (Q4 -3,339.5M, TTM 12.88M against 1,123.5M); NVDA's FY2009-10 rungs in thousands; KO's 2017 and T's 2009 quarters in millions (Q4 16.9B and 23.4B against years of 4.3B and 5.9B); TXN's 2009 nine months in millions. `share_scale` puts the annual record right, and a quarter only where its EPS implies the count, never a derived one or one at or below zero.
Expected: a year's rungs on one scale before Q4 is derived (share_scale's rule applied to the rungs), and no count at or below zero.   Actual: as above.
Impact today: those years' quarterly and TTM counts in the statements tab, the API and the exports; no valuation (the models read annual rows). BX's 2015 and 2017 rungs differ by a restated count basis (1.88x), not a scale.

Fix: before Q4 is derived, a year's weighted-count rungs go onto one scale (`_rungs_on_one_scale`): a rung whose own net income over EPS implies it times a power of 1000 is rescaled by that power (share_scale's rule), provided another of the year's rungs, as filed, is near the result; a rung without a usable EPS takes the scale of its year's checked rungs; and a count at or below zero, filed or derived, is no count and no baseline. The corroboration came from the impact run: a first version took the EPS alone, and TMO's 10-Q for Q3 2009, which tags 2008's nine-month EPS as 1,580,000 (1.58), turned its correct 437.1M into 437.1 and its Q4 into 1.67B; a test of that case went RED first. Six tests, RED on 341f785 (MCD's Q4 2021 -2,223.01M, COP's 0.75M and -2,220M, the zeros), and six mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter66-bug092-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Over the 126 cached filers collected offline (`qa/evidence/iter66-bug092-impact.txt`): 10 change, and no annual or per-share figure; quarterly counts at or below zero 9 -> 0 (basic and diluted), more than 2x off their year's 16 -> 10 (diluted; the rest IPO years, Citigroup's 2009 issuance, BX's restated basis and MS 2010), trailing counts at fiscal year ends more than 10% off the year's 10 -> 4; MCD's Q4 2021 751.5M and Q4 2022 736.3M, COP's Q4 2019 1.10B, KO's Q4 2017 4.32B, NVDA, T and TXN alike, BRK-B's 2009-11 quarters back to its 1.55M class-A-equivalent count from x10^6, CEG's filed zeros empty, BX's negative Q4 2017 empty. The README says so.
### BUG-093 [P1][VAL] A quote refresh after a split puts the post-split price beside valuations on the old basis
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 64)
Found: iteration 62 by code review 9 (C1), re-run in iteration 63 (`qa/evidence/iter63-review9-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 64 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_web_quote_refresh.py::test_a_refresh_after_a_split_since_the_last_collection_keeps_the_stored_quote` (a split on the last collection's day, and one after it beside a later SEC-only run), `::test_a_refresh_without_a_split_since_the_last_collection_saves_the_quote` (five controls), `::test_a_quote_carries_yahoos_split_history`
Repro: the quote job (`src/webapp/jobs.py`: the web app's REFRESH and `POST /api/stocks/{T}/refresh-quote`) calls only `YahooHandler.fetch_quote` and `SQLiteStore.upsert_quote`, which writes one market snapshot. After a split since the last full collection the snapshot moves to the post-split price, while `split_events`, the dividends, the bars and the filed figures stay pre-split: every per-share model sits on the old basis beside the new price, and a backfill can't tell, since nothing records the split. On code review 9's scenario through the real fetcher, YahooHandler and SQLiteStore (a fake yf.Ticker; a 10:1 split on 2026-06-12, the quote refreshed on 06-20): price 101.21, median 526.57, upside +420.3%, "cheap", on the valuation API and the VAL tab, and the same after a backfill; a full collection instead gives median 52.66, upside -48.0%, "expensive" (DDM 41.84, Graham 63.48, Lynch 34.65, multiples 98.34).
Expected: a refresh after a split leaves the price and the valuations on one basis (it brings the split in, or says the ticker needs a full collection).   Actual: as above.
Impact today: none in the user's copy (its 2026 splits predate its snapshots); any split after 2026-08-04, from the first quote refresh after it until the next full collection.

Fix: the quote fetch also reads Yahoo's split history (`_get_splits`, the chart request), and the quote job saves no snapshot when Yahoo shows a split the stored record can't have seen (`SQLiteStore.splits_since_collection`): one missing from `split_events` and dated on or after the ticker's last collection with Yahoo (its run row in `collection_runs`, else its latest price bar), where its splits, bars and dividends come from. The refresh then fails with "Yahoo shows a 10-for-1 split on 2026-06-12 since the last full collection, and the stored valuations are on the old basis, so the quote wasn't saved. Collect FILER in full to update them." Storing the split alone wouldn't do: the dividends and bars stay pre-split, so DDM and the multiples band would be ten times off instead; and a full collection from the refresh would bypass `STOCK_WEB_ALLOW_COLLECTION=0`. Seven tests (two RED on 440d5e7, plus the quote's split history; five controls: the stored split, one before the last collection, one after its last bar but before it, its day's split already stored, a 1-for-1 entry), and seven mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter64-bug093-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). End to end through the app's own refresh route, job manager, YahooHandler and SQLiteStore on code review 9's fake market (`qa/evidence/iter64-bug093-e2e.txt`): the refresh after the split is refused with that message, and the page keeps 1008.86, -47.8%, "expensive" (no row in any of the 19 tables changes); after a full collection, -48.0% on the new basis, and a later refresh saves one market snapshot and nothing else. The README says so. Left: if the split request itself fails, the refresh is saved as before.
### BUG-094 [P2][VAL] When a later run's Yahoo quote fails, its split takes back dividends Yahoo never refetched: DDM ten times its value, and the run says nothing
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 67)
Found: iteration 62 by code review 9 (I1), re-run in iteration 63 (`qa/evidence/iter63-review9-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 67 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_bars_from_before_a_split_stay_on_the_older_prices_basis`, `::test_bars_from_before_a_split_go_onto_a_newer_prices_basis`, `::test_bars_served_on_a_splits_own_day_are_after_it`, `tests/test_yahoo_new_sections.py::test_a_failed_quote_still_brings_the_dividends_with_the_splits`, `::test_a_run_whose_dividends_fail_stores_no_split`
Repro: `unpriced` (BUG-048, BUG-078) multiplies Yahoo's stored dividends and FY-end bars by every split since the price's day, on the premise that the run that stored the split refetched them. When yfinance 1.0's `Ticker.info` raises (quoteSummary and v7/quote failing: a TypeError, or a 429), the quote fails and so does the dividend extractor, which reads `stock.info` before `stock.dividends`, while the split (chart endpoint) is stored and the bars usually refresh. Code review 9's scenario through the real fetcher (a 10:1 split on 2026-06-12, run 2 on 2026-10-01): with `info` raising, a company without SEC annual financials values DDM at 4,183.77 (TTM dividends 219.78) against 418.38, and so does one with ten 10-K years; with the bar request failing too, its multiples band reads 9,833.66 against 983.37. Run 2 records 0 errors and 0 warnings. With `info` empty (BUG-078's own premise) DDM is 418.38, as intended. The oracle shares the predicate, so it agrees.
Expected: Yahoo's series on the price's basis (BUG-048's Expected), and a failed quote or dividend fetch a run warning.   Actual: as above.
Impact today: none in the user's copy; a split between two runs where the second's `info` fails.

Iteration 65 (BUG-084's real-app check, `qa/evidence/iter65-bug084-impact.txt`): the same family with only the price-history request failing after a split, the quote working: the FY-end bars stay on the pre-split basis beside the post-split price, and the multiples band reads 983.37 against 98.34 (DDM, Graham and Lynch right; the median 52.91 holds). The run now warns that the bars weren't refreshed.
Fix: the dividend payments come from the chart, as the splits do, whatever the quote does (`_get_dividend_history` reads `stock.dividends` first and `info` in its own try), and when they fail the run's splits aren't stored either; the bars count as served on the basis of every split up to their last day, which their daily history reaches, and a split between that day and the price's is multiplied in or divided out (`bars_to_price` in `load_inputs`, in place of `unpriced` for the FY-end bars); and a failed quote or dividend history is a run warning, beside BUG-084's for the price history. The oracle took the bar rule first, in its own commit (db4820b), after its premise was probed on real data (every ticker's last bar is 0 or 1 days before its last collection with Yahoo: 112 and 15 of the user's 127, all 18 golden at 1), and it flags the unfixed code on review 9's scenarios (multiples 983.4 against 98.34, 9,834 against 983.4) (`qa/evidence/iter67-bug094-oracle.txt`). Five tests RED on db4820b, and two fixtures of BUG-048's and BUG-060's tests given the day their bars were served, as their premise has it (they pass both ways); a sixth test pins bars served on a split's own day; six mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter67-bug094-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). On review 9's four scenarios through the real fetcher (`qa/evidence/iter67-bug094-impact.txt`): DDM 418.38 where it read 4,183.77, the multiples 983.37 and 98.34 where they read 9,833.66 and 983.37, every failure warned in the run's summary and `collection_runs`, and the oracle agrees (0 beyond); a run whose chart request fails loses its price too (the market data needs that history), so everything stays on the old basis, warned. Nothing moves on the user's copy (0 of 762 valuation rows, 0 of 127 summaries) or the golden basket (0 of 114, 0 of 19). Left: a run on a split's own day before that day's bar would read its bars as from before the split.
### BUG-095 [P2][VALID] A 16-week fourth quarter is never derived: COST and PEP have no Q4 and no trailing year
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 68)
Found: iteration 62 by code review 9 (I2), re-run in iteration 63 (`qa/evidence/iter63-review9-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 68 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_sixteen_week_fourth_quarter_is_derived`, `::test_a_missing_rung_derives_no_two_quarter_figure`, `tests/test_ttm.py::test_a_trailing_year_runs_over_a_sixteen_week_quarter`, `::test_ttm_skips_when_gap_breaks_continuity`
Repro: the ladder derives a quarter only when its increment spans 80 to 100 days (`_QUARTER_MAX_DAYS`), and COST's and PEP's 12-12-12-16-week years end on a 112 to 119-day fourth quarter (COST 2023-05-07 to 2023-09-03: 119 days; PEP 2024-09-07 to 2024-12-28: 112). On 51091ce neither has a quarter labelled Q4 among its 74, and none of their 18 and 17 TTM rows has revenue or net income.
Expected: Q4 = annual - 9-month YTD (VALID-06), and the trailing years the rolling four quarters.   Actual: as above.
Impact today: both tickers' Q4s and every TTM row in the statements tab, the API and the exports; no valuation.

Fix: a quarter runs up to 120 days in the ladder and in the trailing years' step (`_QUARTER_MAX_DAYS`, both modules), which takes a 17-week quarter and still leaves out two quarters, a gap in the rungs, at 160 days or more; the cached filers' only quarters of 101 to 130 days are COST's and PEP's. Two tests RED on 8c34a86 (the 16-week Q4, the trailing year across it); a third guards a missing rung from becoming a two-quarter figure; and the existing gap test, whose assertion could never fail (a window can't end on the third quarter-end left), now pins the window that spans the gap. Four mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter68-bug095-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Over the 126 cached filers collected offline, only COST and PEP change: 17 and 18 fourth quarters where there were none, 64 and 68 trailing years with net income where there were none, and their FY2023-25 quarters' net income adding up to each year's exactly (`qa/evidence/iter68-bug095-impact.txt`).

### BUG-099 [P2][VALID] Revenue's tag order takes a part of the revenue: AXP's ASC 606 contract revenue, and a figure BLK's next 10-K drops
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 70)
Found: iteration 69 via VALID-01 (`qa/evidence/iter69-{revenue,bankfields,sector_rest}.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 70 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_the_total_net_of_interest_expense_is_the_revenue_not_its_contract_revenue`, `::test_a_revenues_figure_the_income_statement_doesnt_add_up_to_gives_way`, `::test_a_revenue_figure_below_its_own_cost_of_revenue_gives_way`, `::test_a_figure_below_cost_gives_way_only_to_one_that_isnt`, `::test_a_zero_revenue_gives_way_to_a_positive_one`, `::test_a_fourth_quarter_is_the_years_revenue_less_nine_months`, `::test_a_filings_vintage_takes_the_years_revenue_not_its_part`, `::test_net_revenue_is_no_noninterest_income`, and six guards beside them
Repro: revenue takes the first of its tags the year has (`Revenues`, the two ASC 606 contract-revenue tags, `SalesRevenueNet`, `SalesRevenueGoodsNet`, then `RevenuesNetOfInterestExpense`). AXP files its income statement's total, total revenues net of interest expense, as `RevenuesNetOfInterestExpense` ($60.52B, $65.95B and $72.23B for FY2023-25, its net interest income plus noninterest income to the dollar), and its contract revenue as `RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax` ($37.22B, $38.83B, $41.30B), which wins. BLK's FY2024 10-K tags `Revenues` of $11.01B and $12.79B for FY2023-24; its FY2025 10-K files those years' total revenue as $17.86B and $20.41B under the contract-revenue tag and no `Revenues`, but `Revenues` wins. Over the 126 cached filers' latest three years (378 filer-years) these five are the only picks under 80% of another revenue tag the filer reports for the year.
Expected: the income statement's total: AXP $60.52B, $65.95B, $72.23B; BLK $17.86B, $20.41B.   Actual: as above, and stored so in the user's data: AXP's FY2025 net margin reads 26.2% (15.0%), BLK's 50.0% for FY2024 then 22.9% for FY2025 (31.2%, then 22.9%), with revenue up 89% (19%).
Root cause: revenue's tag order in `src/mappings/canonical.py`, applied year by year with no check against the filer's other revenue tags or against its latest filing.
Impact today: the shown revenue and the metrics built on it (margins, turnover, growth); no valuation input uses revenue. Graded P2 like BUG-061. Also latent: `noninterest_income` lists `RevenuesNetOfInterestExpense`, which already holds the net interest income, so a bank filing no `NoninterestIncome` would count that twice in its efficiency ratio; no cached filer does.

Fix: revenue's tags put the totals first (`Revenues`, then `RevenuesNetOfInterestExpense`, then the contract-revenue and sales tags), and `XBRLParser._revenue_set_aside` ranks after every other tag a figure that isn't the year's revenue: of two figures 25% or more apart, one the year's income statement doesn't add up to (operating income + operating expenses, or cost of revenue + gross profit) where it adds up to the other; where it adds up to neither, one at or below zero beside a positive one, and one below the year's cost of revenue beside one that isn't. Contract revenue including excise taxes only fills a year with no other figure. The annual record, the vintages and the quarterly ladder's full-year rungs all read it, and a figure set aside still serves a filing that reports no other. `noninterest_income` no longer lists the total net of interest expense. The fix found the same defect at PG (FY2012-14 `Revenues` of $28.4-29.4B beside net sales of $74-82B: gross profit -$9.6B to -$13.0B, Q4s -$31.5B to -$35.1B) and ETN (FY2011-16 `Revenues` of 0: gross profit -$11.3B to -$15.6B, Q4s -$12.0B to -$17.0B). Rule probes over the 126 cached filers' 2,132 filer-years came first: the largest figure would also move MA (its gross revenue before rebates), PM (revenue including excise taxes), D, SO, ADI and GE; filing support would take PM FY2016's gross figure and keep ETN's FY2013-14 zeros; the latest filing's tags would move restatements at DHR, BSX and DUK; the year's income statement told every case apart (`qa/evidence/iter70-bug099-rules.txt`). Eight tests RED on 143cfba, six guards (MA's net revenue beside its gross contract figure, PM's revenue net of excise taxes, figures under 25% apart, a seller below cost, two close totals beside a far part, a filing's own figure in its vintage), and 14 mutants, each failing a test (`iter70-bug099-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). The first digest caught two regressions, vintages that lost their only revenue figure (DELL, MSFT, PM) and a year-wide gate that set aside a total 1% off another; the pairwise rule and the ranking put both right. Over the 126 filers collected offline, only AXP (FY2016-25, now $35.4-72.2B; FY2025's net margin 15.0%, was 26.2%), BLK (FY2022-24), ETN (FY2011-16), PG (FY2012-14) and MS change, MS's Q1-Q2 2018 becoming its net revenues ($11.08B and $10.61B, where a contract-revenue part split them $5.91B and $15.78B); negative revenues fall from 29 quarters and 7 trailing years to 19 and 1, negative annual gross profits from 14 to 5 (`iter70-bug099-impact.txt`). The web app on a scratch database collected offline with the fix shows AXP's FY2025 revenue at $72.23B, BLK's FY2024 at $20.41B and its Q4 2024 at $5.68B (`iter70-bug099-app.txt`). PG's FY2014 Q4 now reads $13.5B, its restated year less nine months as first filed: BUG-062's mix, with the 19 negative quarters left in lead L23.
### BUG-100 [P2][VALID] A derived total liabilities counts the non-controlling interest as a liability: INTC's FY2025 is 14% high
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 73)
Found: iteration 69 via VALID-09 (`qa/evidence/iter69-{derived,sector_rest}.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 73 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_derived_fields.py::test_derived_liabilities_leave_out_the_noncontrolling_interest`, `::test_derived_liabilities_take_total_equity_as_the_parents_when_its_tag_is_unknown`, with the guard `::test_derived_liabilities_beside_equity_that_includes_the_noncontrolling_interest`
Repro: when a filer tags no `Liabilities`, `derived_fields._total_liabilities` fills it as total assets - total equity. But total equity is the parent's (`StockholdersEquity`, its first tag) whenever that is filed, so the non-controlling interest lands in liabilities, against `quality.py`'s own identity (assets = liabilities + equity + NCI, "total_equity is parent-only"). INTC FY2025: assets $211.43B, Intel's equity $114.28B, non-controlling interests $12.08B; the stored liabilities are $97.15B, the filed figures give $85.07B. INTC FY2024: $97.22B against $91.46B; DIS FY2025: $87.64B against $82.90B. Over the 126 cached filers, 266 annual periods get a derived figure with a minority interest inside, high by a median 0.5% and by over 5% in 13 (AMD FY2009 +14.6%, INTC FY2025 +14.2%, MRK FY2008 +9.3%, DIS FY2018 +8.9%). Where liabilities are filed, assets - equity matches them within 0.5% in 1,038 of 1,540 annual periods, and assets - equity - NCI (when the equity is the parent's) in 1,342.
Expected: assets - equity - NCI when the equity is the parent's, and assets - equity when it is `StockholdersEquityIncludingPortionAttributableToNoncontrollingInterest`.   Actual: assets - equity.
Impact today: the shown total liabilities (Financials tab, API, the exports' `sec_total_liabilities`), annual and quarterly; no valuation input. Most remaining gaps are temporary equity (no canonical field) and BUG-063's unmapped NCI tags.
Notes: the derivations otherwise hold: across the 126 filers' annual and quarterly records no reported value changes and every fill is tagged "derived".

Fix: `_total_liabilities` takes out the non-controlling interest too: total assets less `total_equity` less `minority_interest`, except beside equity filed under `total_equity`'s fallback tag, `StockholdersEquityIncludingPortionAttributableToNoncontrollingInterest`, which already holds it (VZ FY2012); equity of no recorded tag is the parent's, as the field defines it. Temporary (redeemable) equity has no field and stays in, as before. Two tests RED on a26e1a7, one guard, four mutants each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter73-bug100-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Over the 126 cached filers collected offline by both versions, 2,769 cells change in 25 tickers (266 annual, 1,018 quarterly, 971 TTM, 514 vintage), each a derived total liabilities less exactly its period's minority interest, nothing else; 13 annual figures move by more than 5% (AMD FY2009 -12.8%, INTC FY2025 -12.4%), and 16 tickers' latest year changes (INTC $97.15B to $85.07B, WMT $185.05B to $178.78B, DIS $87.64B to $82.90B). Against each filer's own figures, the 12 latest years checked now equal its liabilities-and-equity total less its equity including the NCI, to the dollar. Quality's balance identity, which adds the NCI, flags none of the 120 annual periods with reported liabilities beside equity including it (`iter73-bug100-impact.txt`). The web app on a scratch database collected offline shows INTC's FY2025 total liabilities at $85.07B and DIS's at $82.90B in the API and the statements tab (`iter73-bug100-app.txt`).

### BUG-102 [P1][VAL] BUG-093's refusal misses a split after a collection whose quote failed, or all of whose Yahoo requests did: the refresh puts the new price beside old-basis valuations
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 72)
Found: iteration 70 by code review 10 (C1), re-run in iteration 71 (`qa/evidence/iter71-review10-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 72 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_web_quote_refresh.py::test_a_refresh_after_a_split_the_stored_price_predates_keeps_the_stored_quote` (three cases), with guards `::test_a_refresh_after_a_collection_that_priced_the_split_saves_the_quote`, `::test_a_split_yahoo_lists_late_is_refused_after_a_refresh_saved_before_it`, `::test_a_latest_snapshot_without_a_price_leaves_a_stored_split_alone` and `::test_a_ticker_without_a_stored_price_is_checked_against_its_last_collection`
Repro: review 9's fake market (a 10:1 split on 2026-06-12) through the real fetcher, store, engine and the app's `POST /api/stocks/FILER/refresh-quote` (`review10/scripts/scen.py <root> <work> refresh_after_failed_quote`, `_raises`, `_all_fail`). Run 1 (06-01) works. Run 2 (10-01), after the split: (a) its quote fails (empty or raising) and its chart works, so the split and the post-split dividends and bars are stored while the price stays 1008.86 and the valuations stay on its basis; (b) every Yahoo request fails, so nothing of Yahoo's is stored, yet the run row carries `yahoo_finance`. A refresh on 10-02 then saves 103.00.
Expected: one basis, as BUG-093's: the refresh refused, or saved and the ticker revalued; the control, a backfill after (a), gives a median of 52.91, -48.6%, expensive.   Actual: the job reports success, and the API and the VAL tab read "Median fair value $526.57 | Price $103.00 | Upside 411.2%", cheap; in (b) even a backfill stays at +376.4%, cheap, since the split never reaches `split_events`.
Root cause: `SQLiteStore.splits_since_collection` (`src/exporters/sqlite_store.py:766`) never refuses a split already in `split_events`, and dates the last collection with Yahoo by the last run row whose sources name it, which `StockData.merge_yahoo_data` (`src/models/stock_data.py:289`) adds to every fetch without a top-level error, however many of its requests failed. The stored valuations are on the basis of the latest priced snapshot, not of the last run.
Impact today: none in the user's copy; any split after a ticker's last collection, then a collection whose quote (or every Yahoo request) fails, then a refresh, which that run's own warning ("quote unavailable; the stored price wasn't refreshed") invites. Graded P1, as BUG-093, whose refusal it gets past.

Fix: the refusal reads the stored valuations' basis as the engine does, from the latest snapshot's price (`inputs.load_inputs`' `priced_on`): a split dated after that price's day is refused whether or not it is stored, and a split missing from `split_events` from that day, or from the last collection with Yahoo if that is earlier, as before (Yahoo can list a split days after a refresh priced it); with no priced snapshot, from the last collection, as before. The message no longer says "since the last full collection", which case (a)'s collection came after: "Yahoo shows a 10-for-1 split on 2026-06-12 that the stored valuations don't reflect, so the quote wasn't saved. Collect FILER in full to update them." Three tests RED on 4fc00ca (the stored split, every Yahoo request failed, and the same for a split on the price's day) and four guards (a collection that priced the split, a split Yahoo lists late, a latest snapshot without a price, a ticker never priced); BUG-093's seven cases unchanged; seven mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter72-bug102-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Code review 10's three scenarios through the app's own route now refuse the refresh, and the page stays at "Median fair value $526.57 | Price $1,008.86 | Upside -47.8%"; iteration 64's end-to-end check still refuses after the split, then saves once a full collection has priced it (`iter72-bug102-e2e.txt`).
### BUG-103 [P2][VAL] A run whose dividend request fails drops its splits, though its quote and bars are on the new basis: Graham and Lynch ten times their value
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 74)
Found: iteration 70 by code review 10 (I1), re-run in iteration 71 (`qa/evidence/iter71-review10-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 74 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_yahoo_new_sections.py::test_a_run_whose_dividends_fail_keeps_its_splits`, `tests/test_store_migration.py::test_dividends_keep_the_day_yahoo_served_them`, `::test_dividends_stored_before_the_served_day_take_their_series_latest_basis`, `::test_a_migration_interrupted_while_dating_dividends_is_undone_whole`, `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_a_dividend_goes_to_the_prices_basis_from_the_day_yahoo_served_it` (five cases), `::test_dividends_of_a_database_without_served_days_are_on_every_splits_basis`, `::test_a_dividend_without_a_served_day_beside_bars_a_split_behind_is_on_every_splits_basis`, `::test_without_a_price_a_dividend_stays_as_yahoo_served_it`
Repro: the same market, run 2 (10-01) with only the dividends' chart request failing (`scen.py ... combo:dividends_fail`, `dividends_fail_then_ok`): the quote (102.98) and the bars are post-split, but `fetch_all` empties the splits, so nothing records the 10:1 split and the filed per-share figures stay pre-split beside the post-split price.
Expected: the filed figures and Yahoo's series on the price's basis: Graham 63.48, Lynch 34.65, multiples 98.34 (run 3, everything working, gives a median of 52.91, -48.7%).   Actual: Graham 634.76, Lynch 346.50, median 331.42, +221.8%, cheap. With the bars failing too: multiples 983.37, median 490.63, +376.4%, cheap; with the quote failing instead, multiples 98.34 against 983.37 on the old price's basis (median 382.44, -62.1%). Only all three failing reads right.
Root cause: BUG-094's fix (`src/fetchers/yahoo_handler.py:97`) drops the splits whenever the dividend history fails, which only helps when the quote failed too. In yfinance 1.0 a failed chart request isn't cached, so `Ticker.splits` asks again and can succeed (code review 10's `yf_retry.txt`).
Impact today: none in the copy; a split between two runs where the second's dividend request alone fails. A regression of 8c34a86 in the case where the quote and bars work (f0690bc read Graham 63.48 there). Graded P2, as BUG-094.

Fix: the basis of Yahoo's dividends is recorded rather than inferred, as code review 10 proposed. `dividend_events` keeps `served_on`, the day of the collection that fetched each payment, which Yahoo split-adjusts for every split up to that day; a run whose dividend request fails writes none, so the stored ones keep their day. The run keeps its splits (`yahoo_handler.fetch_all`; the warning now reads "the stored dividends weren't refreshed"), and the engine takes each payment to the price's basis by the splits between its served day and the price's (`inputs._dividend_to_price`, the bars' rule payment by payment); a payment without a served day is on every stored split's basis, the rule until now, since until this fix a run stored no split without the dividends from the same chart request. The migration dates the payments stored before the column by the later of their ticker's last bar and latest split, which reproduces that rule, in one transaction committed before the run writes (BUG-083's lesson). The oracle took the same rule first, in its own commits (1ee9fbc, 7191997): on BUG-103's database, with the split and served days stored as the fix stores them and valued by the unfixed engine, the old oracle passes its DDM of 316.33 and the new one derives 31.63; its output is unchanged on the real and golden copies and on code review 10's nine scenario databases (`qa/evidence/iter74-oracle-dividend-basis.txt`). Tests: the fetcher's (BUG-094's split-dropping test, rewritten), three of the store's and eight of the engine's; RED on 3d39b8f (the two engine cases that fail on the old rule: 1.9 for 0.19 and 19.0 for 1.9); eight mutants, each failing a test (`iter74-bug103-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Code review 10's scenarios through the real fetcher, store, engine and the app's route now read as the review expected: with only the dividends failing, Graham 63.48, Lynch 34.65, multiples 98.34, DDM 31.63 on the post-split basis (until the next full run brings the August payment: 42.35), -52.4% expensive where the unfixed code read +221.8% cheap; with the bars failing too, multiples 98.34 (was 983.37); with the quote failing instead, multiples 983.37 and DDM 418.38 on the old price's basis (were 98.34 and 418.38); BUG-102's refusals hold (`iter74-bug103-e2e.txt`). On fresh copies of the user's data and the golden basket, the migration dates every payment (109 and 15 tickers, none by a split), and 0 of 762 and 0 of 114 valuation rows change against the unfixed engine; the oracle still finds the known 8 rows of 480 and 0 of 61 (`iter74-bug103-impact.txt`).
### BUG-104 [P2][VAL] After a split whose run's bar request failed, the market-close rebuild divides post-split dividends by pre-split closes: FY-end prices up to 12.8% low
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 75)
Found: iteration 70 by code review 10 (I2), re-run in iteration 71 (`qa/evidence/iter71-review10-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 75 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_dividends_restated_for_a_split_after_the_bars_go_onto_their_basis` (two cases), `::test_dividends_served_before_a_split_go_onto_the_basis_of_bars_served_after_it`, with the guard `::test_dividends_on_the_bars_basis_stay_as_they_are`
Repro: `review10/scripts/rebuild_after_split.py` and `rebuild_after_split_values.py` on fresh copies of the user's data (from before the dividend-adjusted flag, so every bar is Yahoo's adjusted close): KO splits 2:1 on 2026-09-15, and a later run stores the split and its dividends rewritten on the new basis while its bar request fails (the bars end 2026-08-04, before the split); its quote works or fails.
Expected: the FY-end market closes on the price's basis (halved with the quote, unchanged without it), within VAL's 0.5%.   Actual: FY2016-25 rebuilt 12.80% to 0.66% low either way (FY2021 27.766 against 29.605 with the quote, 55.532 against 59.21 without), and the multiples value 2.48% low (37.515 against 38.470; 75.03 against 76.939). The DDM's -24.52% in the quote case is the simulation's: it adds no September payment.
Root cause: `_yahoo_dividend_factors` (`src/valuation/inputs.py:403`) solves each factor as 1 / (1 + amount x later / close), the amount on the post-split basis and the close on the bars' pre-split one, so each dividend counts at 1/ratio of its size; `bars_to_price` is applied after.
Impact today: transitional: bars stored before BUG-075's fix (all of the user's, until each ticker is re-collected), a split after them, and a later run whose bar request fails; dividend payers only.

Fix: the rebuild puts each payment onto the stored bars' split basis before solving its factor, from the day Yahoo served it (`dividend_events.served_on`, BUG-103's) to the bars' last day, by the splits in between (`_yahoo_dividend_factors` with `_dividend_onto`, the price's rule now taking any day's basis); a payment without a served day is on every stored split's basis, as for the price. The oracle took the same rule first, in its own commit (5c5f7c3): on code review 10's two KO databases, valued by the unfixed code (6715f99), the old oracle passes the multiples band and the new one flags it (37.51 stored against 38.47, and 75.03 against 76.94), and its output is unchanged on 14 other databases (`qa/evidence/iter75-oracle-rebuild-basis.txt`). Three tests RED on 6715f99, both directions (FY2021 rebuilt at 98.02 for 100 with post-split amounts against pre-split closes, 51.98 for 50 the other way), one guard; four mutants, each failing a test (`iter75-bug104-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Code review 10's scenario on fresh copies of the user's data now rebuilds KO's FY2016-25 prices exactly (+0.00% with and without the quote), and the band reads 38.47 and 76.939, as expected; the oracle finds nothing beyond 0.5% (`iter75-bug104-e2e.txt`; the quote case's DDM -24.52% is the simulation's, which adds no September payment). On fresh copies of the user's data and the golden basket, unmigrated and migrated, 0 of 762 and 0 of 114 valuation rows move (`iter75-bug104-impact.txt`).

### BUG-106 [P2][UI] A quote refresh is refused for a stock dividend or a spin-off factor, called a split: SCCO's after nearly every quarter
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 76)
Found: iteration 70 by code review 10 (M2), re-run in iteration 71 (`qa/evidence/iter71-review10-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 76 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_web_quote_refresh.py::test_a_stock_dividend_or_a_spin_off_factor_doesnt_stop_a_refresh` (three cases), `::test_a_split_or_a_large_price_factor_is_refused_by_its_name` (three cases)
Repro: `review10/scripts/stock_dividend_refusal.py` on a fresh copy of the user's data with the real job manager: a new factor after the copy's last collection (2026-08-04) for SCCO (its quarterly stock dividend, 1.01; seven since 2024-08 in the copy), HON (a spin-off, 0.9535) and SPGI (1.057). All three refreshes are refused: "Yahoo shows a 1.01-for-1 split on 2026-08-14 since the last full collection ...", "a 1-for-1.04877 split", "a 1.057-for-1 split".
Expected: only a factor that moves the stored per-share figures (the parser applies simple ratios of 3:2 or more) is refused, and any other is named for what it is.   Actual: every factor but 1.0 is refused as a split.
Root cause: `splits_since_collection` takes every ratio but 1.0; `_split_since_collection` (`src/webapp/jobs.py:48`) words each as a split.
Impact today: SCCO's refreshes refused once its next stock dividend is out, nearly every quarter; HON's and SPGI's around their spin-offs. Graded P2 (a feature degraded for some tickers, with a misleading reason); code review 10 proposed P3.

Fix: only a factor that moves the stored per-share figures stops a refresh, as the valuation reads them (`sqlite_store._moves_per_share`): a share split, which is a simple ratio (`inputs._simple_ratio`, 5:4 and 1:10 too), or any factor of 3:2 or more (`SPLIT_JUMP_THRESHOLD`; ABT's 2.084 for AbbVie). The stock dividends and smaller spin-off factors Yahoo also lists are saved beside, and the next full collection brings their price history and dividends over; for the filed figures a full collection moves nothing for them either. The refusal names a split by its ratio ("a 5-for-4 split", "a 1-for-10 split") and any other factor as a price adjustment ("a 2.0842 price adjustment (a spin-off or merger)"). Five tests RED on a3e9e27 (the three small factors refused; a 5:4 split named "1.25-for-1", ABT's factor a "2.0842-for-1 split"), one guard (a reverse split); five mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter76-bug106-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Code review 10's script on a fresh copy of the user's data with the real job manager: SCCO's, HON's and SPGI's refreshes saved (3 of 3, none refused); the 10:1 refusals of BUG-093 and BUG-102 hold, through the app's own route (`iter76-bug106-e2e.txt`).
Correction (iteration 89, BUG-128): "for the filed figures a full collection moves nothing for them" holds, but a full collection does move the DDM and the multiples band, which are built on Yahoo's prices and dividends, rebased by every factor; a saved refresh now stores such a factor and the ticker is valued on it.

### BUG-110 [P1][STORE] The as-of view loses a year's figures once a later filing repeats only part of it: 70% of years read no revenue as of today
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 78)
Found: iteration 77 via VALID-10, lead L22 (`qa/evidence/iter77-pit.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 78 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_asof_reader.py::test_a_figure_a_later_filing_leaves_out_keeps_its_last_filed_value`, `::test_a_figure_filed_after_the_date_is_never_carried`, `::test_as_of_value_and_history_read_each_figure_as_last_filed`, `tests/test_pit_metrics.py::test_ratios_read_each_figure_as_last_filed`, `tests/test_web_api_asof.py::test_annual_asof_returns_the_figures_a_later_filing_leaves_out`, `::test_asof_result_fragment_dates_a_figure_last_filed_earlier`
Repro: a 10-K repeats only part of each earlier year (the income and cash-flow statements of the two years before, the balance sheet of the one before, the opening balances of its equity statement three years back), and each such filing is stored as a vintage of the year holding just those figures. `AsOfReader.as_of_annual` returns the latest vintage filed on or before the date, whole. So as of 2026-01-01 MSFT's FY2016 is the FY2019 10-K's vintage (filed 2019-08-01), with no revenue or net income, where the FY2018 10-K (filed 2018-08-03) restated them to $91.154B and $20.539B. On a fresh offline collection of the 126 cached filers, as of 2026-12-31, 1,629 of 2,318 (ticker, year)s read no revenue that an earlier filing reported (net income 1,655, operating cash flow 1,664, diluted EPS 1,594, total assets 1,771): 104 of the years two before their filer's latest (the balance sheet) and 82 to 125 at each age from three to eighteen. The user's data alike: 1,627 of 2,312.
Expected: as of a date, each figure as last filed on or before it (the README: fundamentals "as they were known on a given date"): MSFT FY2016 revenue $91.154B as of 2026-01-01.   Actual: None, and so the point-in-time ratios built on it.
Impact: the as-of explorer, `/api/asof/...`, `AsOfReader` and `PointInTimeMetrics` (backtests); no valuation input (the valuation reads the annual record). The rest of VALID-10 holds: 7,711 vintages, one per (ticker, year, filing), none without a filing date; 23,097 as-of answers (each filing day and the days either side) are each the latest vintage filed on or before the day, none filed after it; MSFT FY2016 reads $85.32B until the FY2018 10-K, then $91.154B; the latest years' point-in-time ratios equal the stored ones (2,160 values) and a hand computation (AAPL, JPM and KO FY2024).

Fix: `AsOfReader.as_of_annual` reads every vintage filed on or before the date and takes each figure from the latest one that holds it, so a figure the latest filing leaves out keeps the value last filed. The provenance stays the latest filing's, and `carried_from` names the filing of each figure last filed earlier (`{field: {accn, filed_date, form}}`); the as-of explorer shows that date beside the figure ("Revenue $91.15B (filed 2018-08-03)"), and the README's SQL example reads one figure that way. Six tests RED on 4f768b8 (the figures empty; `carried_from` absent), seven mutants each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter78-bug110-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). On a fresh offline collection and a copy of the user's data, every as-of answer (23,097 and 23,079, each filing day and the days either side, 78 figures) equals an independent reading of the vintages, provenance and carried filings included, and as of 2026-12-31 no figure an earlier filing reported comes back empty (1,629 and 1,627 revenues before); point-in-time ROE exists for 2,074 of 2,318 years (436 before) and 94% of the ratios computed equal the stored ones (95.5% of far fewer before). Of the 73,531 carried figures 99.4% equal the annual record; the rest follow BUG-116's rule or BUG-085's errors (`iter78-bug110-realdata.txt`). Through stock-web on a copy of the user's data, MSFT FY2016 reads $85.32B as of 2017-01-01, $91.154B restated as of 2018-08-15, and $91.154B (filed 2018-08-03) as of 2026-01-01, with net margin 22.5% and ROE 24.7% (`iter78-bug110-e2e.txt`).
### BUG-111 [P2][VALID] A quarter derived from a falling year-to-date total is stored as a positive outflow: INTU's Q4 capex, GE's Q3 2013 acquisitions ($7.8B)
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 80)
Found: iteration 77 via VALID-07 (`qa/evidence/iter77-{quality-sweep,falling-ytd}.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 80 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_falling_year_to_date_outflow_derives_no_quarter`, `::test_an_outflow_tagged_with_both_signs_in_one_year_keeps_its_quarters`; guards `::test_an_outflow_tagged_negative_derives_its_quarters`, `::test_a_quarter_without_an_outflow_stays_zero`, `::test_a_signed_flow_keeps_its_signed_quarters`
Repro: `XBRLParser._collect_discrete_flows` derives a quarter as its year-to-date figure less the one before and, for the sign-normalized flows (capex, acquisitions, buybacks, debt repaid, dividends paid), takes the absolute value of the difference, which is right for filers that tag the outflows negative. When the year-to-date total falls instead, because a later filing reports the flow on another basis, the fall is stored as the quarter's outflow: INTU's 10-Qs give capex of $99M for the nine months to 2025-04-30 and its 10-K $84M for FY2025 (capitalized software, $40M, apart), so Q4 FY2025 is stored as $15M and the quarters sum to $114M against the year's $84M; likewise 16 of its 17 fiscal Q4s since FY2009 (the quality pass's quarterly-sum findings for FY2021-25). Across the 126 cached filers, 200 such quarters at 75 filers (acquisitions 117, debt repaid 37, capex 26, buybacks 19, dividends 1), 198 of them in the user's stored quarters; 55 at 29 filers are at least $10M and 5% of their year: GE's Q3 2013 acquisitions $7.80B, DELL's Q4 FY2022 acquisitions $3.97B (the year's $16M), GE's Q4 2013 capex $3.38B, WDC's debt repaid $1.95B.
Expected: no quarter where the year-to-date total falls (its bases differ, so the quarter can't be read from them).   Actual: the fall, as a positive outflow.
Impact: the shown quarterly flows (Financials tab, API, the exports' quarterly rows) and the TTM series summed from them (`financials_ttm`); no valuation input.

Fix: `_collect_discrete_flows` differences an outflow's year-to-date sizes (`abs(val) - abs(prev)`, where it took the size of the difference) and derives no quarter where the size shrinks; a zero quarter stays. The same root put right a year whose filings tag the outflow with both signs, whose quarter took the two totals' sum: LLY's 10-Qs tag its 2008 dividends negative and its 10-K positive (Q4 $3,598.2M, now $515.2M), GE's 2016 buybacks the other way round ($40,725M, now $4,437M), CVX's Q4 2010 dividends $9,902M (now $1,436M): 54 quarters at 27 filers. Two tests RED on 9e99a02 (INTU's Q4 read $15M; LLY's 3,500 against 500), three guards (outflows tagged negative, a quarter without one, a signed flow); six mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter80-bug111-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Offline over the 126 cached filers: 200 quarters no longer derived and 54 corrected, the five outflows only, with 640 trailing-year cells; the annual record, the vintages' figures and the metrics unchanged; the quality sweep loses INTU's five quarterly-sum findings and nothing else (INTU 50 to 100); the valuation reads no quarterly figure (`iter80-bug111-realdata.txt`). Through stock-web on that collection: INTU's Q4 FY2025 capex shows "—" beside Q1-Q3's $33M, $31M and $35M, LLY's Q4 2008 dividends $515.2M, GE's Q4 2016 buybacks $4.437B (`iter80-bug111-e2e.txt`). The user's stored quarters keep the old figures until their next collection (L20).
Correction (code review 12, I1; re-run in iteration 85, `qa/evidence/iter85-review12-rerun.txt`): the rule is right for the four gross payments, whose negative figures are a sign convention (capex, dividends, buybacks, debt repaid: the 29 changed cells there are right), but acquisitions are net of cash acquired, and a negative year-to-date total there is a net inflow as filed. GE's Q3 2013 $7.80B, cited above as a fall read as an outflow, is a real one (Lufkin and Avio), and 25 of the 54 quarters this fix changed, with 8 it emptied, are acquisitions: BUG-127.

Status: VERIFIED (iteration 81)
Found: iteration 78 by code review 11 (I1), re-run in iteration 79 (`qa/evidence/iter79-review11-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 81 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_store_migration.py::test_a_run_before_new_yorks_open_dates_its_payments_by_its_last_bar`, `::test_a_split_yahoo_lists_dates_the_payments_it_adjusted`, `::test_a_run_without_bars_or_splits_dates_its_payments_the_day_before_its_own`, `::test_dividends_keep_the_day_yahoo_served_them` (now with bars), `tests/test_valuation_inputs.py::test_a_run_before_the_open_on_a_splits_day_keeps_its_payments_pre_split`
Repro: `dividend_events.served_on` is `collected_at[:10]`, the collector's local date (`sqlite_store.py:626`), and `inputs._dividend_onto` counts a split dated on the served day as already in the payment (`day <= served`); the bars are dated by their last bar, New York's day. Code review 11's `scen11.py tz_split_day` on a399dc0: run 1 at local 2026-06-12 08:00 (+0700; New York still on 06-11, so Yahoo serves no split yet) stores the payments with served day 2026-06-12, the split's day; after run 2 (local 06-13 08:00, New York 06-12, post-split; only the dividends' request fails) they stay on the pre-split basis beside the post-split price: DDM 418.38 against 41.84 in the control (run 1 at local 06-11 20:00), median 80.91 against 52.66, upside -20.0% against -47.9%; Graham, Lynch and the multiples are right, and the oracle passes it (0 rows beyond 0.5%, BUG-109). A third run where everything works puts it right.
Expected: payments dated on Yahoo's calendar, as the bars are (the chart response's last day, or `collected_at` in New York's time).   Actual: the local date.
Impact today: none in the copies, but this machine's clock is +0700: 50 of the 166 Yahoo runs in the user's data ran at 14:xx local (03:xx in New York, before the open), and the 15 tickers last collected then have their last bar the day before. It needs a split, Yahoo not yet applying it at that hour, and a later run whose dividend request alone fails. Shares BUG-079's root; a3e9e27's `_dividend_onto` applies the same rule to the market-close rebuild. Graded P2 like BUG-103 (a scenario-only valuation error).

Fix: the store dates a run's payments on Yahoo's calendar (`sqlite_store._served_day`): the run's last daily bar or the latest split Yahoo lists, whichever is later, the rule BUG-103's migration dates older rows by; a run with neither takes the day before its own (Yahoo then lists no split, and one it hasn't applied is dated on New York's day or later). The valuation's rule (`_dividend_onto`) is unchanged. Four tests RED on 3e3b721 (the payments dated 2026-06-12, the collector's day, where Yahoo's was 2026-06-11; the end-to-end payment read 1.9 against 0.19), one guard (a split Yahoo lists dates the payments it adjusted); six mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter81-bug117-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Code review 11's scenario through the real fetcher, store, valuation engine and web app: after run 2, DDM 41.84, median 52.66, upside -47.9%, the control's values (418.38, 80.91, -20.0% before), the payment dated 2026-06-11; the other 11 scenarios of reviews 10 and 11 (failed quotes, failed dividends, the refusals of BUG-093 and BUG-102, the JSON) read as before (`iter81-bug117-e2e.txt`). The oracle passes either reading (BUG-109). Stored rows keep their day: the valuation's read path is unchanged, and only a collection writes payments.
Correction (code review 12, M6): 54 of the 166 Yahoo runs in the user's data ran before New York's open (50 at 14:xx local, 3 at 15:xx, 1 at 19:xx), not 50 as 3e3b721's commit message says. The fallback's reach east of UTC+10:30 is BUG-131.
### BUG-118 [P2][STORE] The JSON export keeps a failed dividend request's error in place of the dividend history
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 82)
Found: iteration 78 by code review 11 (I2), re-run in iteration 79 (`qa/evidence/iter79-review11-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 82 (see LOG) | Regression test: `tests/test_json_exporter.py::test_a_failed_dividend_request_keeps_the_earlier_history`
Repro: code review 11's `scen11.py json_divfail` on a399dc0: run 1 (2026-06-01) writes FILER.json with 46 payments; run 2 (2026-10-01), whose dividends' request alone fails, writes `{"error": "'NoneType' object is not subscriptable"}` and 0 payments in their place, while SQLite keeps all 46. `json_exporter._merge_data` (`:184`) takes an error-only section as data.
Expected: BUG-018's rule: a section the run didn't get keeps the earlier one.   Actual: the history is gone from the JSON until the next good run.
Impact: the JSON's dividend history after every run whose dividend request fails; no valuation reads it. Pre-existing (8c34a86 does the same).

Fix: the JSON merge counts a handler's failure alone, `{"error": ...}`, as nothing the run got (`json_exporter._is_empty`), so the section keeps the earlier data, as every other section a run didn't get does (BUG-018); the run's warning still says the dividends weren't refreshed. Only the dividend history comes back in that shape from a full collection. One test RED on f87638f (the file's history became `{'error': 'boom'}`); three mutants, each failing it (`qa/evidence/iter82-bug118-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Code review 11's scenario through the real fetcher and exporters: after the run whose dividends' request failed, FILER.json keeps its 46 payments (0 before) (`iter82-bug118-e2e.txt`). The kept payments are the earlier run's, on the split basis Yahoo served them on, and the JSON keeps no served day (lead L26).

### BUG-124 [P1][VALID] A fiscal year's first quarter gets no income-statement or cash-flow figures until its second 10-Q: the latest quarter of 61 of the user's 126 companies is empty
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 84)
Found: iteration 83 via MATCH-02 (`qa/evidence/iter83-match-real.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 84 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_a_years_first_quarter_comes_from_its_own_10q_before_the_second`, `::test_a_january_years_first_quarter_is_its_new_years`, `::test_a_year_in_progress_is_the_year_it_starts`, `::test_a_transition_period_a_10k_closes_keeps_its_ends_year`; guards `::test_a_later_quarters_own_3_month_figure_leaves_its_ladder_value`, `::test_a_lone_later_quarters_figure_stays_unread`, `::test_a_lone_first_quarter_figure_makes_no_quarter_of_its_own`
Repro: `XBRLParser._collect_discrete_flows` skips a year-to-date ladder of one rung ("not a cumulative ladder (isolated 3-month fact)"), and a fiscal year's ladder has one rung until its second 10-Q: the first quarter's 3-month figure is the year to date. Synthetic: a first 10-Q with Q1 revenue of 100 alone gives no quarter; with the second 10-Q (250 for six months) Q1 reads 100 and Q2 150. In the user's data the latest quarter of 61 of 126 companies (ended 2026-03 to 2026-05) holds its balance sheet and no revenue, net income or cash flow, and so does the latest trailing year: META's Q1 2026 (10-Q filed 2026-05-01) and its trailing year to 2026-03-31 are empty, the last with revenue ending 2025-12-31 ($200.97B; Yahoo's trailing revenue $228.25B); NVDA's Q1 FY2027 likewise. A fresh collection with today's parser: 62 of 127.
Expected: a first quarter from its own 10-Q.   Actual: nothing until the next one, about three months later.
Impact: the newest column of the quarterly and trailing-year views (Financials tab, API, exports) for every company between its first and second 10-Q of a year; no valuation input.

Fix: `_collect_discrete_flows` reads a year's one-rung ladder, its first quarter's own 3-month figure from the day the year starts, into that quarter, after every ladder so it displaces no derived figure, and only where the filings hold the quarter (its balance sheet: BUG-096's re-dated quarters stay out); other lone 3-month figures stay unread. A year still open now belongs to the year starting on its start day (as its own full-year figure dates it, or the year after the one ending the day before): by its latest end's calendar year a January filer's first quarter, NVDA's to 2026-04-26, was FY2026's, beside FY2026's own; a period a 10-K closes keeps its end's year, as before (NOW's July-December 2011 between its June and calendar years). Four tests RED on eeb061e (META-like Q1 empty; NVDA's Q1 FY2026; a January year in progress FY2026; the transition period after the label rule's first cut), three guards; ten mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter84-bug124-{red,red-labels,red-transition,green,mutants}.txt`). Offline over the 126 cached filers against iteration 80's collection: 1,547 quarterly cells filled in 161 quarters (57 of them Q1 2026, the rest earlier years whose field only the first 10-Q tagged) and 1,424 trailing-year cells, nothing emptied, one value moved (MCD's Q1 2026 basic count from 710.7 to 710.7M: the year's EPS now puts it on its scale); the annual record, vintages and metrics unchanged; the 7 shared (year, quarter) labels as before; empty latest quarters 62 to 1 (XOM2, the new registrant); the quality sweep unchanged; each filer's latest trailing year within 5% of Yahoo's for 96 of 111 revenues (86 before) and 94 of 111 net incomes (67) (`iter84-bug124-realdata.txt`). Through stock-web: META's Q1 2026 revenue $56.31B and trailing year $214.96B; NVDA's Q1 FY2027, its trailing year $253.49B and $159.61B, Yahoo's own (`iter84-bug124-e2e.txt`).
### BUG-125 [P1][VALID] `long_term_debt` misses the tags most filers now use: 22 companies have no long-term debt, so their net debt, debt/EBITDA, ROIC and EV are wrong (ORCL's ROIC 88%)
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 86)
Found: iteration 83 via MATCH-03 (`qa/evidence/iter83-match-real.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 86 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_long_term_debt_is_read_from_the_tag_the_filer_uses` (five tags), `::test_the_noncurrent_tags_win_beside_the_fallbacks`; guard `::test_an_instruments_carrying_amount_is_not_the_years_debt`
Repro: the field reads `LongTermDebtNoncurrent` and `LongTermDebt` only. Of the 36 companies whose latest year has no long-term debt, 22 file it under another tag, at least 2% of their assets: `LongTermDebtAndCapitalLeaseObligations` (VZ $139.5B, SO $65.6B, AVGO $62.0B, CVS $60.5B, COF $49.9B, PM $45.1B, KO $42.1B, CVX $39.8B, RTX $34.3B, XOM $34.2B, AMT $33.8B, COP $22.4B, SCHW $22.2B, APH $14.6B, BSX $11.1B, GLW $7.6B), `LongTermNotesPayable` (ORCL $122.3B), `UnsecuredLongTermDebt` (GS $285.5B), `NotesPayable` (O $25.0B), `DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount` (EQIX $19.1B, PGR $7.0B), `ConvertibleLongTermNotesPayable` (NOW $1.5B), mostly in line with Yahoo's total debt. In the user's data and a fresh collection alike: VZ's total debt $18.6B (Yahoo $193.6B), net debt -$0.4B, debt/EBITDA 0.39, ROIC 21.6% on $105.3B of invested capital; ORCL's debt/EBITDA 0.26, ROIC 88.4% on $18.4B; KO's debt/EBITDA 0.10, ROIC 48.3%; and our EV is about the market cap alone (VZ $191.8B against Yahoo's $389.9B).
Expected: the year's long-term debt from the tag the filer uses (with the current portion, as now, in `short_term_debt`).   Actual: none.
Impact: the shown leverage and return metrics (net and total debt, debt/EBITDA, ROIC and invested capital, EV and the EV multiples) for those 22 companies, on the company tabs and in the screener; no valuation input (the valuation reads no debt).

Fix: `long_term_debt` reads, after `LongTermDebtNoncurrent` and `LongTermDebt`, the noncurrent tags of filers who file neither (`LongTermDebtAndCapitalLeaseObligations`, `LongTermNotesPayable`, `UnsecuredLongTermDebt`, `ConvertibleLongTermNotesPayable`) and last `NotesPayable`, a total of notes (O). `DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount` was measured and left out: often one instrument's (SLB's $0.5B note at 2020-12-31 beside some $16B), on days with no balance sheet, where it made four debt-only quarters at BSX and HWM that cost seven trailing years. Seven tests RED on d56405a, a guard for the left-out tag; five mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter86-bug125-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Through stock-web: VZ's long-term debt $139.53B, debt/EBITDA 3.32, ROIC 9.28% (`iter86-bug125-e2e.txt`).
Correction (code review 13): the commit's "seven tests RED" counts a DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount case that was RED before it became the guard (six RED on base); its "1,587 quarters" are 1,585 by the reviewer's count. The tag order it kept (LongTermDebt second) double counts current maturities: BUG-141.
### BUG-126 [P2][STORE] SQLite's `enterprise_value` is our EV after a collection and Yahoo's after a quote refresh, while the JSON's is Yahoo's: the two differ by more than 5% for 47 of 111 companies
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 87)
Found: iteration 83 via MATCH-03 (`qa/evidence/iter83-match-real.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 87 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_sqlite_store.py::test_a_quote_refresh_keeps_our_enterprise_value`, `::test_a_refresh_at_the_collections_market_cap_reads_its_ev_family`, `::test_a_refresh_without_a_stored_year_keeps_the_collections_ev_family`; guards `::test_a_refresh_carries_no_ev_multiple_ours_doesnt_have`, the latest year in the fixture
Repro: a collection stores `calculated_metrics`' EV (Yahoo's market cap plus the latest year's SEC net debt) and EV/EBITDA in `market_snapshots`; `upsert_quote` stores Yahoo's own `enterpriseValue` and `enterpriseToEbitda` in the same columns (a scratch store: 1,200 and 12.0 after a collection, 5,010 and 50.1 after the refresh). The JSON's `valuation.enterprise_value` is Yahoo's in every run, so the same field disagrees across the stores for all 127 companies of the user's data (AAPL $4.516T against $4.450T, ABT $187.8B against $213.1B). The definitions differ by more than 5% for 47 of 111 companies and more than 20% for 13: about 2x for the banks (JPM $1.03T ours, $474B Yahoo's), and Yahoo's is negative for BRK-B (-$265B) and C (-$346B); BUG-125's missing debt widens the rest.
Expected: one definition per column (ours, or Yahoo's under its own name), in every store and after a refresh.   Actual: the column follows the kind of run, and so do the screener's EV multiples.

Fix: `upsert_quote` computes the snapshot's EV family (`_EV_FAMILY`: EV, EV/EBITDA, EV/FCF, FCF yield) as a full collection's `calculated_metrics` does, the calculator on the latest stored year with the quote's market data and the company's sector (`_ev_family`), where it stored Yahoo's own EV and EV/EBITDA and carried the last collection's EV/FCF and FCF yield to the new price; without a market cap or a stored year the previous snapshot's carry forward. The JSON keeps both under their own sections (`valuation` Yahoo's, `calculated_metrics` ours); the README says so. Three tests RED on 5d2d996 (the refresh stored 5,010 and 50.1 where ours is 1,260 and 10.5; Yahoo's EV where a collection has none), two guards written after the fix (the latest year; no EV multiple carried where ours has none); five mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter87-bug126-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). On copies of the user's data, a refresh at each collection's own market data: before, all 127 tickers' EV family differed from the collection's (112 took Yahoo's EV: AAPL's EV/EBITDA 31.20 to 26.50); after, all 127 equal it (`iter87-bug126-realdata.txt`). Banks' EV family is computed from inputs their sector rules suppress: BUG-134.
### BUG-127 [P2][VALID] BUG-111's rule reads acquisitions by size, but they're net of cash acquired: a quarter after a net inflow is dropped or understated (GE's Q3 2013 $7.80B, COP's Q4 2021 $8.67B)
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 88)
Found: iteration 84 by code review 12 (I1), re-run in iteration 85 (`qa/evidence/iter85-review12-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 88 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_xbrl_parser.py::test_acquisitions_are_net_so_a_total_below_zero_brought_cash_in`, `::test_a_years_acquisition_quarters_add_up_to_its_figure`, `::test_a_lone_first_quarter_that_brought_cash_in_reads_below_zero`, `::test_a_year_whose_acquisitions_brought_cash_in_reads_below_zero`; guards `::test_acquisitions_in_a_filing_that_tags_its_payments_negative_are_turned_back`, `::test_a_10k_that_tags_its_payments_negative_keeps_its_acquisitions_paid`, `::test_a_later_10k_that_flips_a_years_acquisitions_is_put_right`
Repro: since 3e3b721 an outflow's quarter is what its year-to-date total's size grew by, none where the size shrinks. That is right for the four gross payments (capex, dividends, buybacks, debt repaid), whose negative figures are a sign convention, but `acquisitions` is `PaymentsToAcquireBusinessesNetOfCashAcquired`: a negative year-to-date total is a net inflow as filed, and the next quarter is the signed increase. Code review 12's scripts on eb0aec3's collection against iteration 77's: 30 acquisitions quarters at 19 filers (22 changed, 8 emptied) sit beside a negative total in filings that tag their gross outflows positive. GE 2013: Q1 -$6.383B, H1 -$6.187B, nine months +$1.617B, its capex positive in the same 10-Qs, so Q3 grew by $7.804B (Lufkin and Avio, about $7.6B): stored None. COP FY2021: nine months -$382M (cash acquired with Concho), the year +$8.290B, so Q4 $8.672B: stored $7.908B. TSLA Q3 2019 $14M against $76M; CVS Q3 2008 $0.4M against $55.4M; CB Q4 2023 None against $250M. But in filings that tag every outflow negative sizes are right: LLY's Q4 2008 $6,038.6M (signed $6,127.4M), TXN's $0 (signed $38M).
Expected: acquisitions differenced by sign where the filing tags its gross outflows positive (an inflow quarter left empty), by size where it tags them negative.   Actual: by size everywhere.
Impact: about 30 quarters of acquisitions after the next collection (Financials tab, API, exports, trailing years); no valuation input. BUG-111's GE example was this case: a real outflow.

Scope (iteration 88): the annual record and the vintages read acquisitions by size too, so a year that brought cash in was stored as an outflow: 36 years at 23 filers and 90 vintages (WFC's FY2008 -$11.203B, the cash acquired with Wachovia, read +$11.203B; ADI's FY2021 -$2.451B with Maxim; COF's FY2025 -$16.465B with Discover; USB's FY2008-14). Over the cached companyfacts, 126 of the 130 acquisitions rungs below zero that the parser reads sit in filings that tag their gross outflows positive, 86 of them reported at the same sign by another filing (`qa/evidence/iter88-bug127-realdata.txt` sections 1-2).
Decision (iteration 88, revising Expected): a net inflow is stored below zero, not left empty. Acquisitions are a net figure, so a quarter or a year that brought cash in reads as filed and a year's quarters add up to its figure; an empty inflow quarter would have left COP's FY2021 Q4 at $8.672B beside a year of $8.290B, with no trailing year over it.
Fix: `acquisitions` has its own sign rule, `SIGN_NET` (stored as paid: positive pays, negative brings cash in), in place of `SIGN_ABS`. `_net_outflows_as_paid` keeps each filing's acquisitions as filed, except in a filing whose gross outflows (capex, dividends, buybacks, debt repaid) are mostly below zero, where they're turned back (LLY's and TXN's 2008 10-Qs); a tie isn't a convention (GE's 2015-17 10-Ks tag capex positive and buybacks negative, and its 2016-17 ones report acquisitions paid). It runs once per extraction, so the annual record, the vintages, the ladders and the lone first quarters read the same figures, and a later filing's flip of a year (GE's 2015 10-K: FY2012 -$1.456B where two earlier 10-Ks agree on +$1.456B) is put right by BUG-061's repair, which now reports it. Four tests RED on 4c74cbf, three guards (the negative convention for quarters and for a 10-K and its vintage; GE's flip and tie); ten mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter88-bug127-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Offline over 128 cached filers, before and after: only acquisitions change, in 36 years (each now below zero), 180 quarters (41 turned below zero, 117 filled where none was stored, 97 of them below zero, 22 changed, none emptied), 361 trailing years and 90 vintages, plus GE's repair warning; valuations, metrics and every other table identical. ### BUG-128 [P2][WEB] A quote refresh is saved after a spin-off factor under 3:2, beside a DDM and multiples band still on the old basis: upside -34.8% where a full collection reads -39.2%
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 89)
Found: iteration 84 by code review 12 (I2), re-run in iteration 85 (`qa/evidence/iter85-review12-rerun.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 89 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_web_quote_refresh.py::test_a_refresh_after_a_price_factor_stores_it_and_values_the_ticker_on_it` (three cases), `::test_a_refresh_after_a_stored_factor_its_price_predates_values_the_ticker_on_it`; guards `::test_a_refresh_without_a_new_factor_writes_only_the_snapshot`, `::test_a_refused_refresh_stores_no_factor`, `::test_a_failed_valuation_keeps_the_refresh`
Repro: since BUG-106's fix (bce9244) only a share split or a factor of 3:2 or more stops a refresh. A spin-off factor below 3:2 rebases Yahoo's price series like a split of its size, while the stored DDM and multiples band stay on the old series, and the quote job stores no factor, so neither a backfill nor anything short of the next full collection puts them right. Code review 12's `scen12.py` on eb0aec3 (the factor on 2026-06-12, the share count unchanged; a refresh or a full collection on 06-15): 1.253 (GE's 2024 factor): after the refresh price 807.06, DDM 418.38, multiples 983.37, median 526.57, upside -34.8%; after a full collection DDM 333.90, multiples 784.81, median 490.63, upside -39.2%. 1.324 (T's 2022): -31.1% against -35.8%. 6715f99 refused both.
Expected: such a refresh refused beyond a materiality tolerance, or its factor stored and the ticker's valuations recomputed.   Actual: saved beside the old basis.
Impact: one non-simple factor above 5% a year or so in the user's universe (T, WDC, DHR twice, GE twice, MET, HON, SPGI, PFE since 2015); a scenario-only valuation error (graded P2; BUG-102's 10x gap in the same refusal was P1).

Decision (iteration 89): the factor stored and the ticker valued on it, not a tolerance. No tolerance serves both the valuations' (+-0.5% against the oracle) and BUG-106: SCCO's quarterly stock dividends of 1.005-1.01 would be refused again under 0.5%, and a 2% one leaves 1-2% errors standing; storing the factor reproduces a full collection exactly (code review 12's `scen12_recompute.py`).
Fix: the quote job reads every factor since the last collection (`SQLiteStore.factors_since_collection`: the date rule `splits_since_collection` had, for every ratio but 1.0); the share splits and factors of 3:2 or more among them still refuse the refresh (`splits_since_collection`). Any other, a stock dividend or a spin-off, rebases Yahoo's prices and dividends, which the DDM and the multiples band are built on: the saved refresh stores Yahoo's split history with the price (`upsert_quote(..., splits=)`, one transaction, as a collection stores it) and the job values those tickers on it (`engine.compute_and_store`, never-fail like the collection job's). An ordinary refresh still writes only the snapshot (GATHER-13). Four tests RED on ef7cc76, three guards; eight mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter89-bug128-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). Code review 12's fake market through the web app's own refresh route: after GE's 1.253 the refresh reads DDM 333.90, multiples 784.81, median 490.63, upside -39.2%, the full collection's (it read 418.38, 983.37, 526.57, -34.8%); T's 1.324 -35.8% (-31.1%), SPGI's 1.057 -46.1% (-45.0%), SCCO's 1.01 -47.6% (-47.4%), each the full collection's, split_events holding the factor; a 2:1 split still refused (`iter89-bug128-e2e.txt`). On a copy of the user's data a refresh listing each ticker's stored split history finds nothing new for all 127, so nothing more is written; 45 stored factors aren't share splits (SCCO's seven, HON's, GE's, DHR's...) (`iter89-bug128-realdata.txt`).
Code review 14 (I1), re-run in iteration 99 (`qa/evidence/iter99-review14-rerun.txt`): the recompute values DDM's trailing year as of the refresh day on the payments the last collection stored, so after a payment since then it reads low (BUG-160); "as a full collection would" holds only without one.

### BUG-134 [P2][VALID] A bank's EV, EV/EBITDA, EV/FCF and FCF yield are computed from the net debt, EBITDA and FCF its sector rules suppress: JPM's EV $1.03T, FCF yield -15%
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 90)
Found: iteration 87 while fixing BUG-126 (`qa/evidence/iter87-bug126-realdata.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 90 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_sector_metrics.py::test_a_banks_ev_family_goes_with_its_net_debt_ebitda_and_fcf`, `::test_an_insurers_ev_to_ebitda_goes_with_its_ebitda`, `::test_a_reits_fcf_ratios_go_with_its_fcf`; guards `::test_a_general_companys_ev_family_is_computed`, `::test_yearly_metrics_without_a_price_gain_no_ev_keys`
Repro: `CalculatedMetrics.calculate_all` computes the EV family from net debt, EBITDA and FCF before `apply_sector` nulls those three for a bank (`sector_metrics.SUPPRESSED_BY_SECTOR[BANK]`, as not meaningful with deposits for funding), and the EV family isn't suppressed, so the snapshot keeps it. In the user's data every one of the 13 banks has net debt, EBITDA and FCF empty in `metrics_annual` beside an EV family in `market_snapshots`: JPM EV $1,027.5B (Yahoo's $474.2B), EV/EBITDA 12.62, FCF yield -15.35%; BAC $782.1B, 19.55; GS $314.3B, 3.46; MS $572.5B, 14.68.
Expected: the EV family suppressed with its inputs for banks, stored NULL as the README's sector table promises for the EBITDA family ("a screen ... excludes sectors where [it] is undefined instead of returning a misleading value").   Actual: values from the suppressed inputs. Impact: the screener's EV columns and the company page for the 13 banks; no valuation input.

Fix: a ratio built on a suppressed figure goes with it (`sector_metrics.SUPPRESSED_BY_SECTOR`): a bank's whole EV family (EV, EV/EBITDA, EV/revenue, EV/FCF, FCF yield) with its net debt, EBITDA and FCF; an insurer's EV/EBITDA with its EBITDA; a REIT's EV/FCF and FCF yield with its FCF. `apply_sector` nulls only the keys a dict holds, so the yearly metrics, which have no price, gain no EV keys (the README leaves EV ratios out of the as-of metrics); every other suppressed key is always computed first, as before. The README's sector table names them. No valuation model reads the EV family (the snapshot, the STAT page, the JSON and CSV exports do; the web screener whitelists none of it). Three tests RED on 8a90337, two guards; eight mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter90-bug134-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). On copies of the user's data, a refresh of every ticker at its own collection's market data, before and after: 74 cells empty now (13 banks x 4, 8 insurers' EV/EBITDA, 7 REITs x 2), the other 434 identical; JPM's EV $1,027.5B, EV/EBITDA 12.62 and FCF yield -15.35% gone (`iter90-bug134-realdata.txt`). stock-web on each copy: JPM's STAT page reads EV/EBITDA 12.6x, then "—"; CB's EV and FCF yield and O's EV/EBITDA stay (`iter90-bug134-e2e.txt`). The stored snapshots change at the next collection or refresh.
Code review 13 (on ef7cc76, before this fix) measured the same pattern beyond banks: all 8 insurers had an EV/EBITDA beside a suppressed EBITDA (CB's from Yahoo's EBITDA), all 7 REITs an EV/FCF and FCF yield beside a suppressed FCF; the fix covers both.
Code review 14 (M6): of those 74 cells, 8 were already empty in the user's copy (iteration 99's count over the latest snapshots; review 14 counted 9), so ff5da71's "empties 74 cells" counts them too.
### BUG-135 [P1][MATCH] Debt/Equity is Yahoo's percent, shown and screened as a multiple: AAPL's STAT reads 78.4x for 0.78x, and "debt/equity under 2" finds 1 of 111 companies
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 92)
Found: iteration 91 via MATCH-06 (`qa/evidence/iter91-match06-{real,golden,extra}.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 92 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_web_workstation.py::test_stat_shows_yahoos_debt_to_equity_as_the_percent_it_is`, `::test_a_figure_already_in_percent_formats_as_one`, `::test_the_screener_names_debt_to_equity_a_percent`
Repro: `YahooHandler` stores Yahoo's `debtToEquity`, a percent (AAPL's 78.445 is 78.4% of equity), as `market_snapshots.debt_to_equity` (`yahoo_handler.py:307`). STAT's `_STAT_LEVERAGE` formats it as a multiple (`workstation.py:437`: "78.4x"), the screener's kind map does too (`screener.py:134`), and the screener's filter compares the percent: on a copy of the user's data `/api/screen?debt_to_equity_lt=2` returns 1 company (SNDK at 1.503, 1.5%), `_lt=200` returns 94. The stored figures over 111 companies: median 68.9, max 790.9. Of the golden tickers, 13 STAT pages on the user's copy and 15 on the golden copy show the percent as a multiple (KO 115.5x, NEE 161.7x, NVDA 6.6x).
Expected: the ratio (0.78x) on STAT and in the screener, its filter on the same basis, or the figure labelled a percent everywhere.   Actual: 100 times the ratio, as "x".
Impact: STAT's Debt/Equity for every company with Yahoo's figure; the screener's "Debt / equity" filter and column; the snapshot in the exports. No valuation input (`src/valuation` doesn't read it).

Decision (iteration 92): the figure stays Yahoo's percent, shown and named as one, rather than stored as the ratio. The JSON and CSV exports carry it as a percent and USAGE_GUIDE.md's screens read it so (`debt_to_equity < 100`, `{'max': 75}`): converting it would change those exports' unit silently, and the screener compares stored figures as typed. Yahoo presents it as a percent too ("Total Debt/Equity 78.45%").
Fix: `fmt_value` has a "percent" kind, a figure already in percent (78.445 -> "78.4%"); STAT's Debt/Equity and the screener's kind map use it, the screener's filter is labelled "Debt / equity (%)", and the Yahoo field, the README and the guide say it is a percent; the guide's "normalize if over 10" step, which read NVDA's 6.6% as 6.6x, now divides by 100 always. Three tests RED on 3f55659; five mutants, four failing a test, the fifth (the compare view's kind) unreachable, as compare and peers take metrics_annual columns only (`qa/evidence/iter92-bug135-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). MATCH-06's sweep, with the figure judged by its ratio whether shown as a multiple or a percent: Debt/Equity's 13 mismatches on the user's copy and 15 on the golden one are gone, nothing else moved (`iter92-bug135-realdata.txt`). stock-web on a copy of the user's data: AAPL 78.4%, KO 115.5%, NEE 161.7%, NVDA 6.6%; the filter labelled "Debt / equity (%)": under 100 finds 70 companies, under 200 94 (`iter92-bug135-e2e.txt`).
### BUG-136 [P2][UI] Share counts and volume are shown as dollars: AAPL's "Shares outstanding $14.59B", "Volume $18.56M"
Status: VERIFIED (iteration 96)
Found: iteration 91 via MATCH-06 (`qa/evidence/iter91-match06-{real,golden,extra}.txt`) | Fixed: iteration 96 (see LOG) | Regression tests: `tests/test_web_workstation.py::test_a_count_formats_without_a_currency`, `::test_des_and_stat_show_counts_without_a_currency`, `::test_the_statements_hp_holders_and_insiders_show_counts_without_a_currency`
Repro: counts go through `fmt_money`, which prefixes "$" (by design: `workstation.py:420-424` and `pages.py`'s `_STATEMENT_ROWS`, "for consistency"): DES's Volume and Avg Volume, STAT's Shares outstanding, Float shares and Shares short, every statement table's Shares outstanding row (annual, quarterly, TTM) and the HP table's volume. Over the golden tickers on the user's copy, 1,140 cells (15 DES pages x 2, 15 STAT x 3, 152 annual, 471 quarterly and 442 trailing-year cells); on the golden copy, 1,026.
Expected: a count without a currency ("14.59B").   Actual: "$14.59B". The magnitudes and suffixes are right.

Fix: `fmt_count` (and `fmt_value`'s "count" kind) formats a count with fmt_money's B/M/K suffixes and no currency, the two sharing `_suffixed`; every count the app showed as money uses it: DES's Volume and Avg Volume, STAT's Shares outstanding, Float shares and Shares short, the statements' Shares outstanding, the HP table's volume, and the holders' and insiders' shares (the last two found beside the four MATCH-06 named); the comments that called money "consistency" say so no longer. Three tests RED on 96374fa; eight mutants, each failing a test (`qa/evidence/iter96-bug136-{red,green,mutants}.txt`). MATCH-06's sweep, which reads a suffixed count with no currency as a count: the 1,140 and 1,026 dollar-signed counts are gone, every value equal (29,644 on the user's copy, 28,114 on the golden one; `iter96-bug136-realdata.txt`). stock-web on a copy of the user's data: AAPL's DES Volume 18.56M, STAT Shares outstanding 14.59B, statements 14.77B, HP volume 18.56M, Blackrock's 1.14B shares beside $350.76B, an insider's 116 shares beside $34.24K (`iter96-bug136-e2e.txt`).
